§ 10.01 TITLE OF CODE.
   This codification of ordinances by, and for, the City of Holiday Island
shall be designated as the “Code of Holiday Island”, and may be so cited.
§ 10.02 INTERPRETATION.
   Unless otherwise provided herein, or by law or implication required, the
same rules of construction, definition and application shall govern the
interpretation of this code as those governing the interpretation of state law.
§ 10.03 APPLICATION TO FUTURE ORDINANCES.
   All provisions of Title I compatible with future legislation shall apply to
ordinances hereafter adopted amending, or supplementing, this code, unless
otherwise specifically provided.
§ 10.04 CAPTIONS.
   Headings and captions used in this code, other than the title, chapter and
section numbers, are employed for reference purposes only, and shall not be
deemed a part of the text of any section.
§ 10.05 DEFINITIONS.
   (A)   General rule. Words and phrases shall be taken in their plain,
ordinary and usual sense. However, technical words and phrases having a
peculiar, and appropriate, meaning in law shall be understood according to
their technical import.
   (B)   Terms defined. For the purpose of this code, the following definitions
shall apply unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different
meaning.
      CITY, MUNICIPAL CORPORATION or MUNICIPALITY. The City of Holiday Island,
Arkansas.
      CODE, THIS CODE or THIS CODE OF ORDINANCES. This municipal code, as
modified by amendment, revision and adoption of new titles, chapters or
sections.
      COUNTY. Carroll County, Arkansas.
      MAY. The act referred to is permissive.
      MONTH. A calendar month.
      OATH. An affirmation in all cases in which, by law, an affirmation may be
substituted for an oath and, in such cases, the words SWEAR and SWORN shall be
equivalent to the words AFFIRM and AFFIRMED.
      OFFICER, OFFICE, EMPLOYEE, COMMISSION or DEPARTMENT. An officer, office,
employee, commission or department of the municipality, unless the context
clearly requires otherwise.
      PERSON. Extends to, and includes, person, persons, firm, corporation,
copartnership, trustee, lessee or receiver. Whenever used in any clause
prescribing and imposing a penalty, the terms PERSON or WHOEVER, as applied to
any unincorporated entity, shall mean the partners or members thereof, and as
applied to corporations, the officers or agents thereof.
      PRECEDING or FOLLOWING. Next before, or next after, respectively.
      SHALL. The act referred to is mandatory.
      SIGNATURE or SUBSCRIPTION. Includes a mark when the person cannot write.
      STATE. The State of Arkansas.
      SUBCHAPTER. A division of a chapter, designated in this code by a heading
in the chapter analysis, and a capitalized heading in the body of the chapter,
setting apart a group of sections related by the subject matter of the heading.
Not all chapters have SUBCHAPTERS.
      WRITTEN. Any representation of words, letters or figures, whether by
printing or otherwise.
      YEAR. A calendar year, unless otherwise expressed.
§ 10.06 RULES OF INTERPRETATION.
   The construction of all ordinances of the municipality shall be by the
following rules, unless such construction is plainly repugnant to the intent of
the legislative body, or of the context of the same ordinance.
   (A)   AND or OR. Either conjunction shall include the other, as if written
“and/or”, if the sense requires it.
   (B)   Acts by assistants. When a statute, or ordinance, requires an act to
be done which, by law, an agent or deputy as well may do as the principal, such
requisition shall be satisfied by the performance of such act by an authorized
agent or deputy.
   (C)   Gender; singular and plural; tenses. Words denoting the masculine
gender shall be deemed to include the feminine and neuter genders; words in the
singular shall include the plural, and words in the plural shall include the
singular; the use of a verb in the present tense shall include the future, if
applicable.
   (D)   General term. A general term following specific enumeration of terms
is not to be limited to the class enumerated, unless expressly so limited.
§ 10.07 SEVERABILITY.
   If any provision of this code as now, or later, amended, or its application
to any person, or circumstance, is held invalid, the invalidity does not affect
other provisions that can be given effect without the invalid provision or
application.
§ 10.08 REFERENCE TO OTHER SECTIONS.
   Whenever, in one section, reference is made to another section hereof, such
reference shall extend, and apply, to the section referred to as subsequently
amended, revised, recodified or renumbered, unless the subject matter is
changed, or materially altered, by the amendment, or revision.
§ 10.09 REFERENCE TO OFFICES.
   Reference to a public office or officer shall be deemed to apply to any
office, officer or employee of the municipality exercising the powers, duties
or functions contemplated in the provision, irrespective of any transfer of
functions or change in the official title of the functionary.
§ 10.10 ERRORS AND OMISSIONS.
   (A)   If a manifest error is discovered, consisting of the misspelling of
any words; the omission of any word, or words, necessary to express the
intention of the provisions affected; the use of a word or words to which no
meaning can be attached; or the use of a word or words when another word or
words was clearly intended to express such intent, such spelling shall be
corrected, and such word or words supplied, omitted or substituted as will
conform with the manifest intention, and the provisions shall have the same
effect as though the correct words were contained in the text as originally
published.
   (B)   No alteration shall be made, or permitted, if any question exists
regarding the nature, or extent, of such error.
§ 10.11 OFFICIAL TIME.
   The official time, as established by applicable state or federal laws, shall
be the official time within the municipality for the transaction of all
municipal business.
§ 10.12 REASONABLE TIME.
   (A)   In all cases where an ordinance requires an act to be done in a
reasonable time, or requires reasonable notice to be given, REASONABLE TIME OR
NOTICE shall be deemed to mean the time which is necessary for a prompt
performance of such act, or the giving of such notice.
   (B)   The time within which an act is to be done, as herein provided, shall
be computed by excluding the first day and including the last. If the last day
be Sunday, it shall be excluded.
§ 10.13 ORDINANCES REPEALED.
   This code, from and after its effective date, shall contain all of the
provisions of a general nature pertaining to the subjects herein enumerated and
embraced. All prior ordinances pertaining to the subjects treated by this code
shall be deemed repealed from, and after, the effective date of this code.
§ 10.14 ORDINANCES UNAFFECTED.
   All ordinances of a temporary or special nature, and all other ordinances
pertaining to subjects not embraced in this code, shall remain in full force
and effect, unless herein repealed expressly or by necessary implication.
§ 10.15 EFFECTIVE DATE OF ORDINANCES.
   (A)   The effective dates for ordinances of a general or permanent nature
and other local measures of a general or permanent nature of cities of the
first class, cities of the second class and incorporated towns shall be upon
publication or posting as is otherwise required by law, but not before 91 days
after passage by the governing body of the city or town.
   (B)   In the event that the governing body of the city or town has by
ordinance fixed the deadline for filing referendum petitions upon ordinances or
other local measures at not less than 30 days, nor more than 90 days, after
passage of an ordinance or measure, then the effective date shall be the day
next following the deadline fixed in the ordinance.
      (C)   (1)   An ordinance containing an emergency clause shall go into
effect immediately upon passage or at the time specified by the emergency
clause, regardless of publication or posting, but an emergency clause shall not
be effective to impose any fine, penalty, forfeiture or deprivation of liberty
or property until after the ordinance has been published or posted as is
otherwise required by law.
      (2)   The effective date of an ordinance that is not of a general or
permanent nature of a city of the first class, city of the second class or
incorporated town is the date of passage of the ordinance unless a later
effective date is provided in the ordinance.
Statutory reference:
   Related provisions, see A.C.A. § 14-55-203
§ 10.16 REPEAL OR MODIFICATION OF ORDINANCE.
   (A)   Whenever any ordinance or part of an ordinance shall be repealed, or
modified, by a subsequent ordinance, the ordinance or part of an ordinance thus
repealed or modified shall continue in force until the due publication of the
ordinance repealing or modifying it when publication is required to give effect
thereto, unless otherwise expressly provided.
   (B)   No suit, proceedings, right, fine, forfeiture or penalty instituted,
created, given, secured or accrued under any ordinance previous to its repeal
shall, in any way, be affected, released or discharged, but may be prosecuted,
enjoyed and recovered as fully as if the ordinance had continued in force,
unless it is otherwise expressly provided.
   (C)   When any ordinance repealing a former ordinance, clause or provision
shall be itself repealed, the repeal shall not be construed to revive the
former ordinance, clause or provision, unless it is expressly provided.
§ 10.17 ORDINANCES WHICH AMEND OR SUPPLEMENT CODE.
   (A)   If the legislative body shall desire to amend any existing chapter or
section of this code, the chapter or section shall be specifically repealed,
and a new chapter or section containing the desired amendment, substituted in
its place.
   (B)   Any ordinance which is proposed to add to the existing code a new
chapter or section shall indicate, with reference to the arrangement of this
code, the proper number of such chapter or section. In addition to such
indication thereof, as may appear in the text of the proposed ordinance, a
caption or title shall be shown in concise form above the ordinance.
§ 10.18 SECTION HISTORIES; STATUTORY REFERENCES.
   (A)   As histories for the code sections, the specific number and passage
date, of the original ordinance, and amending ordinances, if any, are listed
following the text of the code section. Example: (Ord. 10, passed 5-13-1960;
Ord. 15, passed 1-1-1970; Ord. 20, passed 1-1-1980; Ord. 25, passed 1-1-1985)
   (B)   (1)   If a statutory cite is included in the history, this indicates
that the text of the section reads substantially the same as the statute.
Example: (A.C.A. § 14-55-504) (Ord. 10, passed 1-17-1980; Ord. 20, passed 1-1-
1985)
      (2)   If a statutory cite is set forth as a “statutory reference”
following the text of the section, this indicates that the reader should refer
to that statute for further information. Example:
§ 39.01 PUBLIC RECORDS AVAILABLE.
   This municipality shall make available to any person for inspection or
copying all public records, unless otherwise exempted by state law.
Statutory reference:
   Inspection of public records, see A.C.A. §§ 25-19-101 et seq.
§ 10.99 GENERAL PENALTY.
   (A)   General penalty established. If an ordinance or code does not
specifically provide for the penalty for violating that ordinance or code, then
this section, along with the below-mentioned fines, will encompass those
violations. This section is not designated to supersede any ordinance or code
in which penalties have already been established. Any conflict between the
penalties stated below, and any other ordinance or code violations will be
resolved by deference to the more specific ordinance or code violation.
   (B)   Maximum penalties permitted.
      (1)   The city shall not inflict any fine or penalty by ordinance or
otherwise, to a greater sum than $1,000 for any one specified offense or
violation of a bylaw or ordinance, or double that sum for each repetition of
such offense or violation.
      (2)   If a thing prohibited or rendered unlawful, is, in its nature,
continuous in respect to time, the fine or penalty, for allowing the
continuance thereof, in violation of the bylaw or ordinance, shall not exceed
$500 for each day that it may be unlawfully continued.
      (3)   If any bylaw or ordinance provides for any greater fine, penalty or
forfeiture than is provided in this section, it shall, and may be, lawful, in
any suit or prosecution, for the recovery thereof, to reduce it to such amount
as shall be deemed reasonable and proper, and to permit a recovery, or render a
judgment, for such amount as authorized.
(A.C.A. § 14-55-504)
CHAPTER 11: CITY STANDARDS

§ 11.01 CITY WARDS, BOUNDARIES.
   The city shall be organized into three wards according to the map and legal
descriptions below:
   (A)   Ward #1. Beginning at the intersection of the municipal boundary and
Pleasant Ridge Drive, follow the municipal boundary west. Continue to follow
the municipal boundary clockwise to a point directly north of the intersection
of Shields Drive and Bandy Drive on the Island. From that point, go south to
said intersection to Woodsdale Drive to Green Meadow Drive to Larkspur Lane to
Hillside Drive to Stateline Drive to Holiday Island Drive to Pleasant Ridge
Drive to the starting point;
   (B)   Ward #2. Start at the point on the municipal boundary directly north
of the Woodsdale Drive bridge. From that point, go south to Woodsdale Drive to
Green Meadow Drive to Larkspur Lane to Hillside Drive to Stateline Drive to
Holiday Island Drive to Pleasant Ridge Drive to the intersection of the
municipal boundary and Pleasant Ridge Drive. Follow the municipal boundary
(starting out east) to Highway 23. Follow Highway 23 to Holiday Island Drive.
Follow Holiday Island Drive to Hawk Lane to Hawk Drive to Wild Turkey Drive to
Valley Drive to Pearl Drive to Starlite Avenue to Saturn Avenue to Summit Drive
to Skyview Drive to the north municipal boundary (Arkansas/Missouri state
line). Follow the north municipal boundary west to the starting point; and
   (C)   Ward #3. Beginning at the intersection of Skyview Drive and the north
municipal boundary (Arkansas/Missouri state Line) follow Skyview Drive to
Summit Drive to Saturn Avenue to Starlite Avenue to Pearl Drive to Valley Drive
Wild Turkey Drive to Hawk Drive to Hawk Lane to Holiday Island Drive to Highway
23. Cross Highway 23 to the intersection of Highway 23 and the municipal
boundary. Follow the municipal boundary until the boundary again intersects
with Highway 23. Follow Highway 23 west (south) to the intersection of the
municipal boundary going north. Follow the municipal boundary to the northern
municipal boundary (Arkansas/Missouri state line). Follow northern municipal
boundary west to the starting point.
(Ord. 2021-007, passed 10-19-2021)
§ 11.02 CITY LOGO.
   (A)   The following logo shall be adopted as the official logo for the city.
   (B)   The city logo will have no commission, department or committee
designation as a part of the logo.
   (C)   Any individual, commission or department wishing to use the city logo
must have prior approval from the Mayor.
(Res. 2024-011, passed 10-22-2024)
§ 11.03 DESIGNATED NEWSPAPER.
   The “Carroll County News Midweek”, (USPS-519-220), published weekly by
Carroll County Newspapers in Berryville, AR, shall be designated as the
“newspaper of general circulation” in the city and used for the publishing of
required notices of the city and its authorized commissions.
(Ord. 2022-011, passed 7-19-2022)

GENERAL PROVISIONS
§ 30.01 COMPENSATION; ASSIGNING PROPERTY.
   (A)   Salaries for employees.
      (1)   Effective 1-1-2026, all city personnel salaries and wages shall be
fixed by the Mayor and presented to the City Council as part of the annual
budget which may be amended from time to time.
      (2)   A schedule of current salaries will be kept on file with the
Recorder-Treasurer and available for public inspection.
   (B)   Salaries for elected officials. Effective for the year beginning 1-1-
2026 and ending 12-31-2026, the Mayor, Recorder-Treasurer and City Council
members will be compensated at the following rates:
 
City Council members $0 annually
Mayor                $2,000 monthly
Recorder-Treasurer   $2,520 monthly
 
   (C)   City property.
      (1)   The city department head or supervisor shall be required to get a
signed receipt listing all such property from any person provided with or
furnished any type of property. The receipt shall be filed in the city office
with the Recorder-Treasurer.
      (2)   In the event of death, termination or resignation and the agent,
employee or officer has wages, accrued sick time, vacation, paid leaves of
absence or any other sums due him or her from the city, the city office shall
notify said individual or estate administrator or executor of same in writing
of the amount owed and simultaneously request said individual to return any and
all property supplied or furnished by the city.
      (3)   If the individual has lost, misplaced or otherwise disposed of said
property the city will be paid in full by the individual, his or her executor
or administrator. In the event of depreciable property, the city shall receive
the depreciated value.
      (4)   The city may withhold from wages, accrued sick time, vacation, paid
leave of absence or other sums held by the city, the value of the property not
returned at the time of death, termination or resignation.
(Ord. 2026-001, passed 1-9-2026)
§ 30.02 CODE OF CONDUCT.
   (A)   Conduct in public meetings. Practice civility and decorum in all
discussions and debate. Municipal officials will not make belligerent personal,
impertinent, slanderous, threatening, abusive or disparaging comments. No
shouting or physical actions that could be construed as threatening will be
tolerated. Therefore:
      (1)   Avoid personal comments that could offend other municipal
officials;
      (2)   Honor the role of the Mayor and chairpersons of boards/commissions/
committees in maintaining order;
      (3)   Be respectful of other members of the town council, boards,
commissions, committees, city staff and the public by refraining from abusive
conduct, personal charges or verbal attacks;
      (4)   Treat fellow municipal officials with the respect he or she expects
to receive in return;
      (5)   Always address citizens, employees and fellow municipal officials
with respect;
      (6)   Request the opportunity to speak and address the Council/board/
commission/committee through the Mayor or other person conducting the meeting;
      (7)   Recognize fellow municipal officials and guest speakers by their
formal names and appropriate titles; after initial acknowledgment, the terms
“Mr.” or “Ms.” may be used;
      (8)   Be respectful of others’ time and be brief and prepared in making
remarks; and
      (9)   Be prompt in attending public meetings so that they may begin at
the publicly posted time.
   (B)   Conduct with the public. Be polite and professional to speakers.
Municipal officials should remember that their behavior will either relax the
speaker or push their emotions to a higher level of intensity. Therefore:
      (1)   Limit comments to issues and avoid personal attacks;
      (2)   Remain seated when someone is speaking at the podium;
      (3)   Give the appearance of active listening;
      (4)   Ask for clarification, but avoid debate and argument with the
public;
      (5)   Do not engage in personal attacks of any kind under any
circumstance;
      (6)   Make no promises on behalf of the Council/board/commission/
committee or staff; and
      (7)   Make no personal comments about other municipal officials.
   (C)   Affirmation. Municipal officials are required to affirm commitment to
this policy at the beginning of their term or appointment and all elected
municipal officials are required to reaffirm commitment annually.
(Ord. 2021-003, passed 5-18-2021)
§ 30.03 AI POLICY.
   The city hereby adopts the below Artificial Intelligence Policy, which
defines the authorized use of AI and automated decision tools by city
employees.
   (A)   Purpose. This policy establishes guidelines for the authorized and
prohibited use of artificial intelligence (AI) and automated decision tools by
employees and contractors of the city, in compliance with Act 848 of 2025, the
same being A.C.A. § 25-1-128.
   (B)   Definitions. For the purpose of this section, the following
definitions shall apply unless the context clearly requires a different
meaning.
      ARTIFICIAL INTELLIGENCE (AI). A machine-based system that, based on a
given set of human-defined objectives, can make predictions, recommendations or
decisions influencing real or virtual environments.
      AUTOMATED DECISION TOOL. A system or service that uses AI and has been
specifically developed and marketed, or specifically modified, to make or to be
a controlling factor in making consequential decisions.
      EMPLOYEE. Any person employed by the city.
   (C)   Authorized use.
      (1)   AI and automated decision tools may be used to enhance efficiency,
accuracy and service delivery in city operations.
      (2)   All consequential decisions involving AI tools must be reviewed and
finalized by a human employee or authorized designee.
      (3)   AI tools must not be used to make autonomous decisions without
human oversight.
   (D)   Prohibited use. In accordance with Section (d) of Act 848 of 2025, AI
and automated decision tools shall not be used by city employees to:
      (1)   Express personal political opinion to an elected official unless
within the scope of the employee’s job duties or upon request by an elected
official or public entity;
      (2)   Engage in lobbying an elected official on a personal opinion if the
employee is not a registered lobbyist for the city;
      (3)   Engage in illegal activities or activities otherwise prohibited by
federal or state law; or
      (4)   Intentionally override or avoid the security and system integrity
procedures of the city.
   (E)   Protecting personal information. Employees must never input personally
identifiable information (PII) into AI tools. This includes, but is not limited
to:
      (1)   Names of individuals;
      (2)   Social Security numbers;
      (3)   Driver’s license or ID numbers;
      (4)   Home addresses or phone numbers;
      (5)   Email addresses;
      (6)   Medical or financial records;
      (7)   Employee or student ID numbers; and
      (8)   Photos of individuals without consent.
   (F)   Training.
      (1)   All employees using or interacting with AI tools must complete
training on AI and this policy prior to using AI on city business or equipment.
      (2)   Training shall be provided through the Arkansas Municipal League’s
ACE HUB’s AI Training or other approved sources.
   (G)   Disciplinary action. Violations of this policy may result in
disciplinary action, up to and including termination, in accordance with the
city’s personnel policies and procedures.
   (H)   Acknowledgment. Employee’s shall sign the following acknowledgment: “I
acknowledge that I have read and understood the Artificial Intelligence Policy.
I agree to follow all rules, responsibilities and guidelines outlined in the
policy, including the appropriate and ethical use of artificial intelligence
tools. I understand that violations of this policy and/or failure to comply
with this policy may result in disciplinary action, up to and including
termination.”
(Ord. 2025-018, passed 3-17-2026)
OFFICERS AND EMPLOYEES
§ 30.15 PROSECUTING ATTORNEY.
   (A)   The position of Prosecuting Attorney for the city is created and is to
be distinct and different from the position of City Attorney.
   (B)   The position of Prosecuting Attorney be appointed by the Mayor with
approval of the City Council.
   (C)   The Prosecuting Attorney is not required to be a resident of the city.
   (D)   Compensation will be on an hourly basis with the rate of compensation
to be negotiated by the Mayor.
(Ord. 2022-015, passed 10-4-2022)
§ 30.16 CITY ATTORNEY.
   (A)   The qualifications, election and term of the office of City Attorney
shall be as provided by state law and, effective immediately, the office of the
City Attorney shall be an appointed office. The City Attorney shall be
permitted to engage in the private practice of law during his or her term of
office.
   (B)   The appointment of the City Attorney shall be made by the Mayor with a
majority vote of the members of the City Council. Said appointment will be on
an annual basis starting in 2021, running January 1 through December 31.
   (C)   The person so appointed shall have all the duties as prescribed by
state law and such other duties as may be prescribed by proper ordinance of the
City Council.
   (D)   The City Council recognizes the need for representation and advice
regarding legal matters for the city and finds that the appointment of a City
Attorney will best ensure the town a manner in which to provide for continuous
and competent representation. The City Council finds that this section is
required to ensure the health, safety and welfare of the citizens and
government of the city.
(Ord. 2021-001, passed 3-23-2021)
§ 30.17 CITY COUNCIL MEMBERS; ELECTIONS.
   (A)   Two Council members shall be elected per ward from candidates that
reside in the ward from which the candidates seek to be elected.
   (B)   A.C.A. § 14-44-103(b)(1)(A) shall be adopted for the method of
election of City Council members.
   (C)   Both Council member Number 1 and Number 2 from each ward shall be
voted upon by the qualified electors of the city at large (citywide).
(Ord. 2021-015, passed 11-30-2021)
§ 30.18 RECORDER-TREASURER.
   (A)   The positions of Recorder and Treasurer for the city are to be
combined into one position called Recorder-Treasurer; the qualified electors of
the city shall elect said Recorder-Treasurer every four years on a cycle
commencing in 1972 in accordance with state statute.
   (B)   (1)   The Record Retention Policy and any and all amendments, are
hereby adopted by reference and incorporated herein as if set out in full. The
Record Retention Policy sets forth the requirements to properly preserve the
records of the city in a manner consistent with all pertinent state statutes.
      (2)   The Recorder-Treasurer shall retain and copy city records following
the requirement of the A.C.A. § 14-2-204 and these records will be maintained
and made available upon request per the Arkansas Freedom of Information Act
statutes, A.C.A §§ 25-19-101 through 25-19-110.
(Ord. 2026-011, passed 5-26-2026; Ord. 2026-013, passed 6-16-2026)
Statutory reference:
   Related provisions, see A.C.A. §§ 14-44-114 and 14-44-115
ORGANIZATIONS
§ 30.30 ADVERTISING AND PROMOTION COMMISSION.
   (A)   The city’s Advertising and Promotion Commission is established.
   (B)   The city’s Advertising and Promotion Commission (“Commission”) is
hereby established. The said Commission shall be composed of seven members, as
follows.
      (1)   Four members shall be owners or managers of businesses in the
tourism industry in the city. The owner or manager may reside outside the city,
but shall reside within the county. At least three of the four shall be owners
or managers of hotels, motels or restaurants, and shall serve for staggered
terms of four years; two members of the Commission shall be members of the
governing body of the city, who shall be selected by the City Council, and
shall serve at the will of the City Council; and one member shall be from the
public at large, who shall reside in the city and shall serve for a term of
four years.
      (2)   The initial members of the Commission shall be selected as follows.
         (a)   The four tourism industry positions provided for in division (B)
(1) above shall be nominated by the Mayor and approved by a majority vote of
the City Council for staggered terms so that one member will serve for a term
of one year, one member will serve for a term of two years, one member will
serve for a term of three years; and one member will serve for a term of four
years. The industry positions will be allocated to the one-, two-, three- and
four-year terms based on Mayor decision.
         (b)   The two members of the Commission who are members of the
governing body of the city shall be selected by a majority vote of the City
Council.
         (c)   The at-large position provided for in subdivision division (B)
(1) above shall be selected by the Mayor with the concurrence of a majority
vote of the City Council.
      (3)   Vacancies on the Commission, whether resulting from the expiration
of a regular term or otherwise, in any of the four tourism industry positions
or the at-large position shall be filled by appointment made by the remaining
members of the Commission with the approval by a majority vote of the City
Council. The City Council may remove any member of the Commission by a two-
thirds vote upon 30 days’ notice of intent to take such vote and prior to such
vote occurring.
      (4)   Each member of the Commission shall file with the City Recorder-
Treasurer an oath as required by law in the state of public officials.
      (5)   Each member of the Commission will file with the City Recorder-
Treasurer a statement of financial interest as required by law in the state of
public officials.
   (C)   (1)   The Commission shall meet within two weeks of its appointment
and shall be organized by electing a Chairperson, Secretary and Treasurer.
Thereafter, the Commission shall meet as often as may be necessary and shall
also be subject to special call by the Chairperson of the Commission. The
Commission shall meet at least quarterly.
      (2)   The Commission shall have the authority to employ the necessary
personnel to assist in the administration and operation of the city’s
Advertising and Promotion Fund or may contract with appropriate firms or the
city to perform functions of administration and operations.
      (3)   The Commission shall make quarterly reports to the Mayor and City
Council beginning three months after taking their office, reporting in full on
the operations of the Commission, including an accounting of receipts and
disbursements and shall, upon request of the Mayor or City Council, furnish
such other and further reports and data as may be required. The Commissioners
shall select a recognized auditing firm to submit an annual audit of the
operations of the Commission to the Mayor and City Council.
(Ord. 2024-005, passed 4-16-2024)
§ 30.31 POLICE DEPARTMENT.
   (A)   There shall be established for the city a Police Department, reporting
to the Mayor.
   (B)   The Mayor has the authority to staff the Police Department within the
constraints of the annual budget passed and approved by the City Council.
   (C)   If necessary, in lieu of staffing and equipping the Police Department,
the Mayor of the city is authorized to enter into a contract with the county to
have the County Sheriff Department provide law enforcement services.
   (D)   Said contract must be approved by the City Council prior to signing.
(Ord. 2022-012, passed 11-22-2022)
§ 30.32 BOARD OF ADJUSTMENT.
   (A)   Title. The title of this board is “Holiday Island Board of
Adjustment”.
   (B)   Creation and appointment. The Holiday Island Board of Zoning
Adjustment (“Board of Adjustment”) is hereby established, which shall be the
five members of the Holiday Island City Planning Commission, appointed by the
Mayor and approved by the City Council. Members of the Board of Zoning
Adjustment, prior to adoption of this change in membership, that are not
Planning Commissioners shall continue to serve to the end of their appointed
term on the Board of Zoning Adjustment.
   (C)   Organization. The Board of Adjustment shall adopt rules necessary to
the conduct of its affairs and in keeping with the provisions of these
regulations. Meetings shall be held on a regular schedule, typically on the
same date as the Holiday Island City Planning Commission meeting, in keeping
with the adopted Planning Calendar, and at such other times as the Board of
Adjustment may determine. All meetings shall be open to the public. The Board
of Adjustment shall keep minutes of its vote, indicating such fact, and it
shall keep records of its examinations and other official actions, all of which
shall be a public record. A quorum of the Board of Adjustment shall three
members. The concurring vote of a majority of the total Board of Adjustment
members shall be necessary to revise any order or decision of the Building
Inspector, or to decide on any matter upon which it is required to pass. The
Building Inspector shall attend each meeting of the Board of Adjustment and
shall bring all plans, specifications, plats and papers relating to any case
before the Board of Adjustment for determination.
   (D)   Powers and duties. The Board of Adjustment shall have all the powers
and duties prescribed by law and by these regulations, which are more
particularly described as follows:
      (1)   Administrative review. To hear and decide appeals where it is
alleged there is error in any order, requirement, decision or determination
made by the Building Inspector in the enforcement of these regulations. The
Board of Adjustment may affirm or reverse, in whole or in part, said decision
of the Building Inspector;
      (2)   Variances. To authorize upon appeal, in specific cases, such
variance from the terms of
Chapter 152 of this code of ordinances as will not be contrary to the public
interest where, owing to special conditions, a literal enforcement of the
provisions of these regulations would result in unnecessary hardship that would
deprive the owner of any reasonable use of the land or building involved. A
variance from the terms of
Chapter 152 of this code of ordinances, shall not be granted by the Board of
Adjustment unless and until:
         (a)   The applicant demonstrates that special conditions and
circumstances exist which are peculiar to the land, structure or building
involved and which are not applicable to other lands, structures or buildings
in the same district; that literal interpretation of the provisions of these
regulations would deprive the applicant of rights commonly enjoyed by other
properties in the same district under the terms of these regulations; that
special conditions and circumstances do not result from the actions of the
applicant; and that granting the variance requested will not confer on the
applicant any special privilege that is denied by the zoning regulations to
other lands, structures or buildings in the same district;
         (b)   No non-conforming use of neighboring lands, structures or
buildings in the same district, and no permitted or non-conforming use of
lands, structures or buildings in other districts shall be considered grounds
for the issuance of a variance;
         (c)   The Board of Adjustment shall further make a finding that the
reasons set forth in the application justify the granting of the variance and
that the variance is the minimum variance that will make possible the
reasonable use of land, building or structure;
         (d)   The Board of Adjustment shall further make a finding that the
granting of the variance will be in harmony with the general purpose and intent
of
Chapter 152 of this code of ordinances and will not be injurious to the
neighborhood or otherwise detrimental to the public welfare;
         (e)   In granting any variance, the Board of Adjustment may prescribe
appropriate conditions and safeguards that it deems necessary or desirable. Any
prescribed conditions or safeguards for granting any variance shall become part
of the approved building permit. Violations of such conditions and safeguards,
when made a part of the terms under which the variance is granted, shall be
deemed a violation of these regulations; and
         (f)   Under no circumstances shall the Board of Adjustment grant a
variance to allow a use not permissible under the terms of
Chapter 152 of this code of ordinances in the district involved, or any use
expressly, or by implication, prohibited by the terms of these regulations in
said district.
      (3)   Special exceptions. In addition to the powers and duties specified
above, the Board of Adjustment shall also have the following powers and duties
to hold public hearings and decide the following special exceptions:
         (a)   Interpret zoning district boundaries, upon appeal of the
Building Inspector’s determination and where uncertainty exists, as to the
boundaries of the zoning districts, or when the street or property lines
existing on the ground are at variance with those shown on the zoning district
map;
         (b)   Determine the amount of parking required for a use not listed
herein; and
         (c)   Vary the parking regulations by not more than 25% where it is
conclusively shown that the specific use of a building would make unnecessary
the parking spaces otherwise required by these regulations.
   (E)   Appeals. Appeals from the decisions of the Board of Adjustment shall
be first heard by the City Council in a public hearing.
(Ord. 2022-002, passed 1-18-2022; Ord. 2026-017, passed 8-17-2026)
§ 30.33 PLANNING COMMISSION.
   (A)   Establishment. Pursuant to A.C.A. Title 14, Chapter 56, Subchapter 4,
as amended, the City Planning Commission is hereby created and shall be bound
by, and operate under, and in accordance with the requirements and conditions
of said statute.
   (B)   Planning and Zoning Recommendations to the Mayor and the City Council.
Following the completion of adequate studies, through discussion and
appropriate public input and disclosure, the City Planning Commission
(“Commission”) shall submit recommendations regarding development and
redevelopment policies, general and specific plans, ordinances and regulations
to the Mayor and Council. It is the intent that this planning process be
continuous and that the Commission will be an ongoing part of the decision-
making process relative to development and redevelopment activity in the city.
Such studies and recommendations may be initiated by the Commission or may be
requested by the Mayor and Council.
   (C)   Composition, disclosures, terms, attendance and compensation of
Commissioners.
      (1)   Pursuant to A.C.A. § 14-56-404(a)(2), the City Council may elect to
allow the City Council to serve as the Commission and Zoning Board of
Adjustment by ordinance, approved by a majority vote of the elected City
Council members. Pursuant to A.C.A. § 14-56-406(a)(2), the Mayor shall then
serve as the Chair of the Commission.
      (2)   Alternatively, pursuant to A.C.A. § 14-56-404(a)(1), the members of
the Commission (“Commissioners”) shall be comprised of Commissioners, all
appointed by the Mayor with the advice and consent of the City Council. Four
Commissioners shall be appointed at large, of whom shall not hold any municipal
office or appointment, except membership on the Zoning Board of Adjustment. One
Commissioner may be appointed from the City Council members. All Commissioners
shall be residents of the city and continue throughout the first Commissioners,
from at large, shall remain in office and their terms of office shall be
revised by extending the ends of each of their terms to December 31.
Henceforth, Planning Commission members, that are appointed at large, shall
serve for a term of three years, ending on December 31. Planning Commissioners
will continue to serve beyond the end of their term until a Commissioner is
duly appointed to fill the position. Commissioners may serve more than one
term. The City Council member appointed to the Planning Commission shall serve
at the discretion of the City Council.
      (3)   All Commissioners must disclose annually all real estate holdings
in the city and in the city’s planning area, and any business or financial
interest which could affect, or be affected by decisions of the Commission. All
Commissioners are required to file an annual disclosure report with the City
Recorder-Treasurer pursuant to A.C.A. § 21-8-703, as may be amended from time
to time. No two Commissioners shall be related by blood or marriage in the
third degree, nor shall any two Commissioners have direct financial
involvement. All Commissioners shall have a demonstrated interest, experience
or expertise in land use planning.
      (4)   (a)   Terms of appointment shall begin on June 1. Commissioners
appointed at large, unless appointed to fill an unexpired term, shall be
appointed to serve a term of three years and may be reappointed to three-year
terms. The Council member appointed to the Commission shall serve for the
duration of term as City Council member and may be reappointed if re-elected.
All Commissioners whose terms may expire shall serve until their successors in
office have been appointed and confirmed by the City Council. Any position
vacated by a Commissioner shall be filled by the same procedure and for the
period remaining in the term of that Commissioner.
         (b)   The first at-large appointed Commissioners shall begin with
their appointments, as soon as practical, and have terms expiring as follows:
            1.   One Commissioner having a term that expires on 5-31-2022;
            2.   One Commissioner having a term that expires on 5-31-2023; and
            3.   Two Commissioners having terms that expire on 5-31-2024.
      (5)   (a)   Commissioners who fail to attend at least 70% of all meetings
of the Commission within a 12-month period of service shall be deemed to have
resigned from the Commission and that person’s position shall be deemed vacant
by the City Council.
         (b)   Such Commissioner may petition the City Council for
reinstatement by filing a letter with the City Recorder-Treasurer with ten days
after notification that the citizen’s position has been declared vacant and
explaining why extenuating circumstances justify reinstatement.
      (6)   The Commissioners shall serve on the city’s Planning Commission
without compensation.
   (D)   Commission meetings. Regular meeting dates, at least one to be held in
each calendar month, shall be established by the Commission and published in
the manner required by Arkansas Code provisions. The Commission shall adopt
rules for its own procedure. All meetings shall be open to the public and be
subject to Arkansas Code provisions for elected and appointed officials. An
accurate record of all meetings shall be kept, the original being filed in a
journal in the City Recorder-Treasurer’s office as a permanent record. Audio
recordings of all meetings shall be made and filed with the City Recorder-
Treasurer’s office.
   (E)   Secretarial services. A competent secretary shall be provided by the
city for the purpose of taking notes and other measures at all meetings of the
Commission and composing and typing them in order to construct an accurate
record of proceedings and actions. Said Secretary shall also assist the
Chairperson and others on the Commission in preparing reports, agendas,
correspondence, regulations and other documents or activities that are
essential to Commission business. The Secretary shall be under the direction of
the City Recorder-Treasurer.
   (F)   Appropriation of funds. The Commission cannot expend or encumber city
funds without the prior appropriation by the City Council or within the limits
granted the Mayor of the city.
(Ord. 2021-002, passed 5-18-2021; Ord. 2023-019, passed 8-15-2023; Ord. 2024-
012, passed 9-17-2024)
CHAPTER 31: FISCAL AFFAIRS; FINANCE AND REVENUE
Section
   
31.01   Purchases
   
31.02   Municipal employees doing business in city
   
31.03   City credit card usage
   
31.04   Purchase of real estate
   
31.05   Electronic fund disbursement
§ 31.01 PURCHASES.
   (A)   Purchases of $35,000 or under. The Mayor or his or her duly authorized
representative shall have the exclusive power and responsibility to make
purchases of all supplies, apparatus, equipment, materials, services,
subscriptions, memberships and other things requisite for public purposes for
the city and to make all necessary contracts for work or labor to be done or
material or other necessary things to be furnished for the benefit of the city
or in carrying out any work or undertaking of a public nature where the amount
of the expenditure for any such purpose or contract does not exceed the sum of
$35,000.
   (B)   Purchases over $35,000. Where the amount of expenditure for any
purchase or contract exceeds the sum of $35,000, the Mayor or his or her duly
authorized representative shall invite competitive bids thereon by legal
advertisement in a newspaper of general circulation in the city. Bids received
pursuant to said advertisement shall be opened and read on the date set for
receiving said bids, in the presence of the Mayor or his or her duly authorized
representative. The contract shall be awarded to the lowest responsible bidder;
provided, however, the Mayor or his or her duly authorized representative, may
reject any and all bids received. All contracts must be approved by the City
Council.
   (C)   Approval to make payments. The Mayor, or his or her duly authorized
representative, may approve for payment out of any funds previously
appropriated for that purpose, or disapprove any bills, debts or liabilities
asserted as claims against the city, when funds on hand are adequate to pay
such bills, debts or liabilities. That payment or disapproval of any bills,
debts or liabilities not covered by a previous appropriation shall require
confirmation of the governing body.
   (D)   Competitive bidding. The requirement for competitive bidding is waived
as provided by A.C.A. §§ 14-58-104 and 14-58-303.
(Ord. 2023-017, passed 8-15-2023)
§ 31.02 MUNICIPAL EMPLOYEES DOING BUSINESS IN CITY.
   The Mayor, Council members, elected officials, appointed commissioners and
municipal employees may conduct business with the city as authorized by A.C.A.
§ 14-42-107(b) under the following conditions:
   (A)   The purchase is under $10,000 and comparable merchandise or equipment
at comparable prices is not otherwise available; and
   (B)   The cost of the service is under $10,000 and comparable service at
comparable prices is not otherwise available.
(Ord. 2023-017, passed 8-15-2023)
§ 31.03 CITY CREDIT CARD USAGE.
   (A)   The Recorder-Treasurer is authorized to obtain credit cards in the
name of the city. The Recorder-Treasurer and Mayor will be the primary holders
of such credit cards, but are authorized to allow other city officials or
employees to use the card under close supervision and exclusively for
authorized city business.
   (B)   All applicable laws, city resolutions and ordinances and procedures
relative to making municipal purchases and accounting for those purchases apply
when making credit card purchases. A receipt documenting any credit card
purchase is required. The credit card billing statement is not satisfactory
justification of a credit card purchase.
   (C)   Credit card balances must be paid in full each month in order to avoid
deficit spending.
(Ord. 2023-017, passed 8-15-2023)
§ 31.04 PURCHASE OF REAL ESTATE.
   (A)   Except as hereinafter provided, no purchase of real estate, with or
without improvements, shall be made by the City Council until an appraisal of
said property is made by a licensed appraiser.
   (B)   It is not the intent of this section to establish the price to be paid
for a tract of real estate by requiring that the real estate be appraised, but
rather to be used as an aid and guide by the City Council in arriving at a fair
and just price to pay for the real estate.
   (C)   The requirements of this section may be waived by the City Council in
cases of emergency or in cases where the City Council finds it impractical to
comply herewith.
(Ord. 2023-017, passed 8-15-2023)
§ 31.05 ELECTRONIC FUND DISBURSEMENT.
   (A)   A municipal official or employee responsible for the disbursements for
the city shall have authority to disburse electronic funds payments directly
into payee accounts in financial institutions when necessary for payment of any
expense allowed against the municipality.
   (B)   The disbursement of the city funds shall include adequate supporting
documentation for the disbursement.
   (C)   The electronic funds payment method used by the city shall provide for
internal accounting controls and documentation for audit and accounting
purposes recommended by the state’s Legislative Audit-Information Systems Best
Practices.
   (D)   The electronic funds payment method established by this section shall
not be implemented unless it has been approved by the Legislative Joint
Auditing Committee.
(Ord. 2023-026, passed 10-24-2023)
CHAPTER 32: REFERENDUM PETITIONS
Section
   
32.01   Filing referendum petitions
   
32.02   Notice of publication; posting
   
32.03   Special election
   
32.04   Defeating ordinances and resolutions; procedure
§ 32.01 FILING REFERENDUM PETITIONS.
   All referendum petitions under Amendment No. 7 to the Constitution of the
state must be filed with the Recorder within 30 days after the passage of any
ordinance or resolution.
(Ord. 2021-009, passed 8-24-2021)
§ 32.02 NOTICE OF PUBLICATION; POSTING.
   Upon the filing of said referendum petition, the Mayor is hereby directed to
give notice by publication for one insertion in a newspaper having a general
circulation in the city and by posting in five public places in the city of a
time not less than five days after the publication of such notice at which the
City Council will hear all persons who wish to be heard on the question whether
such petition is signed by the requisite number of petitioners. At the time
named, the City Council shall meet and hear all who wish to be heard on the
question, and its decision shall be final unless suit is brought in the Circuit
Court of the county within 30 days to review its action.
(Ord. 2021-009, passed 8-24-2021)
§ 32.03 SPECIAL ELECTION.
   If the City Council finds that such petition is signed by the requisite
number of qualified petitioners, it shall order a special election to determine
by a vote of the qualified electors whether the ordinance or resolution shall
stand or be revoked, and fix a date which shall be not less than ten days after
the date of the action of the City Council calling the election. The Mayor
shall give notice of the call of such election by publication in not less than
one issue of a newspaper having a general circulation in the city and by
posting in five public places in the city not less than five days prior to the
date of the election. Such notice shall designate by its number, caption and
date of passage, the ordinance which has been referred to the people for
approval or rejection by their vote at such election. Otherwise, subject to the
provisions of Amendment No. 7 to the Constitution of the state and other
applicable laws, said election shall be conducted in the manner provided by law
for the conduct of a regular municipal election.
(Ord. 2021-009, passed 8-24-2021)
§ 32.04 DEFEATING ORDINANCES AND RESOLUTIONS; PROCEDURE.
   If any ordinance or resolution referred to the people is defeated at the
polls, the Council shall make note of such fact and shall expunge the ordinance
or resolution from its files by erasing the same with red ink.
(Ord. 2021-009, passed 8-24-2021)
TITLE V: PUBLIC WORKS
   Chapter
      
50.   SOLID WASTE AND RECYCLING
CHAPTER 50: SOLID WASTE AND RECYCLING
Section
   
50.01   Regulations created; contractor; franchise fee

50.03   Franchise fee

50.06   Sign-up and payment of fees
   
50.07   Disposition of funds

50.09   Burning and dumping solid waste
   
50.10   Collection; bulky items; excluded and hazardous waste
   
50.11   Waste transport

50.13   Inspectors

50.99   Penalty
§ 50.01 REGULATIONS CREATED; CONTRACTOR; FRANCHISE FEE.
   (A)   This regulation establishes a system for the collection and disposal
of solid waste for the city as required per A.C.A. §§ 8-6-201 et seq.
   (B)   (1)   The City of Holiday Island is a member of the Carroll County
Solid Waste Management District (CCSWMD). The County Solid Waste Management
District has, by statute, exclusive rights as stated in A.C.A. §§ 8-6-712 to
“Provide by rule or other legal means, that no person other than as may be
designated by the District, shall engage in the collection or utilization of
solid waste within the District which would be competitive within the purposes
or activities of the ‘District’.”
      (2)   The city engages the County Solid Waste Management District to use
its statutory authority within the city limits and will not license any
contractor or contractors not approved by the CCSWMD.
(Ord. 2026-009, passed 6-16-2026) Penalty, see §
50.99
§ 50.02 MANDATORY CITY COLLECTION SERVICE.
   All structures in the city for which the Holiday Island Suburban Improvement
District provides a water meter or in which persons reside or where any
business, industrial, commercial or other enterprise is operated shall use the
refuse pick up services provided for by this chapter.
(Ord. 2026-009, passed 6-16-2026) Penalty, see §
50.99
§ 50.03 FRANCHISE FEE.
   The City Council reserves the right to award an exclusive franchise for
residential and commercial services and to impose a franchise fee. In such a
case, no other person or entity except such franchisee contractor or his or her
licensee shall be permitted to convey or transport solid waste or recycle
within the city. Such franchise fee shall be payable according to terms set
forth in an agreement between the contractor(s) and the city. Franchise fees do
not apply to services considered to be supplemental per the CCSWMD.
(Ord. 2026-009, passed 6-16-2026)
§ 50.04 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly requires a different meaning.
   ACCOUNT SERVICE FEE. A fee for customers that have “vacation” or “part-time”
homes will be charged a minimum monthly rate per the agreement with the
contractor.
   APARTMENT HOUSE. A single detached dwelling designed for and occupied by two
or more families living independently of each other as separate housekeeping
units.
   BAGS, RESIDENTIAL. Any bag made of plastic, paper or other material that is
used to dispose of solid waste, that does not exceed 35 gallons in volume
capacity, nor 50 pounds in weight, when full and that can be tied shut to
contain solid waste.
   CAN, RESIDENTIAL. A trash can or solid waste storage container with a tight-
fitting lid that does not exceed 35 gallons in volume capacity, nor 50 pounds
in weight, when full, and that is used and owned by a residential solid waste
customer for the purpose of containing solid waste, whether loose, in
residential bags or extra bags as defined above.
   CITY. The municipal corporation and the geographic area within the City of
Holiday Island corporate limits.
   COMMERCIAL CONTAINER. A container that is designed to be mechanically tipped
for unloading, that is between two and eight cubic yards in volume capacity and
that is provided by the District. It is used to contain solid waste materials
on-site at the customer location prior to collection and transfer.
   CONSTRUCTION AND DEMOLITION (C&D) WASTES. Construction and demolition debris
such as building materials, wires, plumbing fixtures, lumber scrap, masonry
scrap, drywall, roofing and similar materials.
   COLLECTION AREA. The area or space in which customers place materials for
collection. Residential curbside COLLECTION AREAS must be located within five
feet of the traffic way used by the collection vehicle to service the customer.
Commercial and industrial COLLECTION AREAS shall generally be located on
private property. The customer is responsible for providing an area for waste
and for waste containers that will support the movement of District vehicles,
will permit all-weather access, and allows sufficient room for collection
vehicles to maneuver without undue hardship to the District.
   HAND LOADED SOLID WASTES. Commercial solid wastes in bags or in trash cans
which are not kept for collection in a Commercial Container as defined above
and that must be loaded by hand where space for a Commercial Container is not
available, or the volume does not justify the use of a Commercial Container.
   HAZARDOUS WASTES. Wastes, in any amount, which are defined, characterized or
designated as hazardous by the United States Environmental Protection Agency or
appropriate State Agency, by or pursuant to federal or state law, or wastes, in
any amount, which is regulated under federal or state law. For purposes of this
chapter, the term HAZARDOUS WASTES shall also include motor oil, combustible
fluids and materials, liquid paint, automotive batteries and waste tires.
   LOW VOLUME/ELDERLY/OR DISABLED ACCOUNTS. Residential accounts which by
reason of age, physical disability or similar problems, may be approved on a
case-by-case basis by the District for special pricing and services. Such a
residence shall have no more than two occupants whose household meets low-
income guidelines as established by the District and that produces minimal
quantities of solid waste (less than 15 gallons) per week.
   POLYCART. A wheeled plastic cart with an integral lid which is specifically
designed to work with District collection trucks, and which is provided to
customers by the District for the purpose of containing solid waste.
   RECYCLABLE MATERIALS. Materials such as newsprint, unwaxed corrugated
cardboard, office paper, clear glass bottles and jars, aluminum and steel cans,
scrap steel, plastic bottles, household hazardous wastes, tires or other
materials that are separated by customers to be diverted from disposal for
beneficial re-use.
   REGULATED MEDICAL WASTE (RMV). Waste generated in health care, research or
laboratory settings that can transmit infectious diseases. It includes
infections pathogens, blood-soaked materials, pathological waste, sharps and
isolation waste.
   RESIDENTIAL CUSTOMER. Any solid waste producer that lives in a structure
that is used primarily as living quarters, where a family or individual uses at
least 75% of the floor space for residential purposes, and where the structure
is not attached to another residence separated by a wall or doors that lock. A
duplex or building with two or more separate residences and with two or more
separate water meters, may be classified as “residential” for purposes of solid
waste pick up.
   RESIDENTIAL UNIT. A dwelling within the corporate limits of the City of
Holiday Island occupied by a person or persons comprising not more than one
family.
   SOLID WASTE. Any trash, food, refuse, container or other rubbish produced by
a residential or non-residential establishment that can be collected and
transported by the District and accepted by a landfill that is duly licensed
and approved by the State of Arkansas and that meets all applicable local,
state and federal laws.
   STREAMLINING. Companies or individuals who, as part of the service they
provide, streamline trash from several residential locations into one
container.
   WHITE GOODS AND BULKY WASTE. Includes common household appliances,
furniture, mattresses, tools, large toys or parts thereof that are too large to
be placed in a 35-gallon bag or can and that are larger than a bundle two feet
by four feet in size.
   YARD WASTE. Includes normal organic yard and garden refuse such as tree,
shrubs or brush trimmings, grass and other plant cuttings, dead plants, trees,
leaves and the like.
(Ord. 2026-009, passed 6-16-2026)
§ 50.05 SOLID WASTE FEES.
   Fee schedules for solid waste collection are to be set by and adjusted from
time to time within the parameters of the agreement with the contractor.
(Ord. 2026-009, passed 6-16-2026)
§ 50.06 SIGN-UP AND PAYMENT OF FEES.
   The contractor shall be responsible for collection of all fees.
   (A)   All customers as designated in §
50.02 must contact the approved Contractor(s) and select a desired level of
service.
   (B)   Any customer who shall remain at the premises and fail to or refuse to
pay all charges within the time provided for by the contractor, the payment
shall be deemed to be delinquent and in violation of this ordinance and subject
to the respective penalties specified in the ordinance. Plus, any delinquent
account penalties charged by the contractor.
   (C)   Residents of the city who own or manage a business located in the city
for which they provide a dumpster can utilize the business dumpster for their
residential trash and not have to sign up for residential service.
   (D)   Part-time residents who will not be at their residence on trash
collection day can utilize a dumpster provided by the city. Trash being put in
these dumpsters must contain a bag tag purchased from the city. The city will
advise as to the location of the dumpsters when purchasing the bag tags. The
city will maintain a record of bag tag purchases. Any part time resident
wishing to use bag tags must sign up for the service with the city.
(Ord. 2026-009, passed 6-16-2026) Penalty, see §
50.99
§ 50.07 DISPOSITION OF FUNDS.
   All fines assessed and paid under the provisions of these regulations,
except those portions required to be paid out for other purposes, shall be
deposited in the city’s General Fund.
(Ord. 2026-009, passed 6-16-2026)
§ 50.08 CONTAINERS.
   (A)   Residential.
      (1)   All residential units shall set out solid waste and recycling for
collection by a contractor in approved containers. All loose waste material is
to be properly bagged and placed in approved containers in order to facilitate
the collection of materials without items being disbursed by blowing wind or
scattered by animals. Unless special arrangements are made, trash is to be
placed at the end of the drive after 5:00 p.m. on the day before collection and
trash containers are to be retrieved and stored no later than 10:00 p.m. on
collection day. This requirement applies to all trash items collected.
Violation of such requirement will result in one warning notice given by the
Code Enforcement Officer. After one notice is given to a resident, subsequent
violations will be referred to the Code Enforcement Officer, and the resident
will be subject to the penalties prescribed in this chapter.
      (2)   Permanently placing or leaving a trash container at the end of a
driveway is strictly prohibited unless approved by the City Code Enforcement
Officer. Such approval will require that the trash container be enclosed in a
structure that is substantially secured and anchored, prevents animals, birds
or weather from dispersing trash, does not block a ditch or impede road right
of way maintenance, does not create a litter problem, does not emit offensive
odors and the appearance of which is not detrimental to adjacent properties.
      (3)   Approval will be dependent on the degree of hardship associated
with placing and removing the trash container from the end of the drive on a
weekly basis. Simple inconvenience is not considered sufficient hardship.
   (B)   Commercial. Solid waste generated by a commercial or industrial
establishment or a multiple residential unit, business or combined units of
businesses (including B&Bs, VRBOs and other forms of short-term rentals) must
utilize a dumpster provided by the contractor. The dumpster is to be located
within the city. A business entity responsible for streamlining multiple
sources of waste into one dumpster must provide the city with a list of active
addresses being serviced. Overflow of the dumpster is expressly prohibited and
no other materials (paint cans, electronics, cardboard and the like) are to be
placed or stored in or around the dumpster.
   (C)   Dumpsters in residential areas. If a dumpster is to be kept in a
residential area it must be kept out of view of passing traffic and cannot emit
any offensive odor. Dumpsters must be kept closed such that rainwater, birds or
animals are prevented from entering the dumpster. Leaking of any liquid from
the dumpster to the ground is expressly prohibited.
   (D)   Part-time residents. Part time residents who will not be at their
residence on trash collection day can utilize a dumpster provided by the city.
Trash being put in these dumpsters must contain a bag tag purchased from the
city. The city will advise as to the location of the dumpsters when purchasing
the bag tags. The city will maintain a record of bag tag purchases and notify
the contractor accordingly. Any part time resident wishing to use bag tags must
sign up for the service with the city.
   (E)   Regular demolition and construction waste. Contractors hauling their
own construction or demolition waste (roofing, house construction and the like)
in their own containers, trucks or trailers must dispose of such waste at the
County Solid Waste District disposal site and demonstrate that the
transportation vehicle will adequately contain the debris during transit.
   (F)   Major demolition and other. Contractors that demolish and remove major
structures such as resulting from fire or condemnation must dispose of the
rubble as approved by the County Solid Waste District.
   (G)   Non-land fill debris and other uses. Construction or other debris that
does not require landfilling (dirt, rocks and the like) or moving non-waste
items (furniture, equipment and the like) and is being transported using a
dumpster or roll off container, are not bound by this chapter.
   (H)   Blocking of a roadway. At no time can a dumpster, roll-off container
or trailer be placed or parked on the road or in any way impede the flow of
traffic, the visibility of drivers at an intersection or the passage of
emergency vehicles.
(Ord. 2026-009, passed 6-16-2026) Penalty, see §
50.99
§ 50.09 BURNING AND DUMPING SOLID WASTE.
   It shall be a violation of these regulations for such owner, occupant,
tenant or lessee of any dwelling place, unit or business in the city, or any
other person, to dispose of solid waste or recycling waste, construction waste
and industrial waste, by dumping, burning or discharging said waste on any
property within the city except through the use of approved services as
provided for in these regulations or specifically authorized by state law or
authority. If a person is found to have disposed of solid waste in a manner not
allowed by ordinance, he or she shall be guilty of a violation of these
regulations and punished as provided for in these regulations.
(Ord. 2026-009, passed 6-16-2026) Penalty, see §
50.99
§ 50.10 COLLECTION; BULKY ITEMS; EXCLUDED AND HAZARDOUS WASTE.
   (A)   Collection. Collection schedules will be published by the hauler.
Notifications of changes to the schedule due to weather, holidays or any other
interruption will be made by the hauler.
   (B)   Bulky item collection.
      (1)   The following regulations governing the collection and disposal of
bulky waste are hereby adopted as follows: a special service shall be provided
for the collection and disposal of major appliances, furniture and other bulky
waste in accordance with the following requirements.
         (a)   Residential customers of the city approved contractor are
allowed two curbside bulky item collections per year on days designated by the
contractor. Bulky item collection is limited to three items per pick up.
Apartment complexes and other multi-family housing units consisting of more
than four units shall not be eligible.
         (b)   All residential customers will be advised by the contractor of
the collection dates and times.
         (c)   Bulky waste shall not be placed curbside until the day prior to
the scheduled collection, but not later than 7:00 a.m. the day of collection.
All items must be placed within five feet of a city street and out of the
street/roadway. All items must be accessible to the collection vehicle and
shall not be obstructed by vehicles, mailboxes, trees/limbs or any other
physical barrier. There must be at least 20 feet of overhead clearance above
the items to be collected.
         (d)   All items must be separated and placed as instructed. Solid
waste items presently collected by the contractor shall not be mixed with bulky
items. Should solid waste be mixed in with bulky waste, no service shall be
extended. Excluded solid waste, extraordinary materials and hazardous waste are
prohibited from being collected and must be disposed of by the owner or
responsible party in an approved manner. The contractor reserves the right to
deny collection of any and all bulky waste item(s).
         (e)   This service will not be extended to persons who are not current
customers in good standing of the contractor providing the service.
      (2)   Fees may be billed for major appliances. Charges will be placed on
the residential customer’s monthly bill per the approved rates.
   (C)   Excluded and hazardous waste. The contractor shall publish a list of
white goods and bulky waste items that are acceptable for collection. The
contractor will also publish a list of electronic equipment and other excluded
materials or hazardous wastes that are not acceptable for pick up and inform
the residents of the proper disposal options.
(Ord. 2026-009, passed 6-16-2026)
§ 50.11 WASTE TRANSPORT.
   Any person operating any truck or other vehicle to transport litter, waste,
trash or garbage within the city shall take reasonable steps to prevent its
contents from blowing, dropping, falling off, or otherwise departing from the
vehicle or attached utility trailer. Such reasonable steps shall include a
secure cover or tarp if reasonably necessary to prevent the contents from
blowing, dropping, falling off or otherwise departing from the vehicle or
utility trailer. However, no vehicle hauling predominately metallic material
shall be required to be covered if it is loaded in a manner which will prevent
the material from falling or dropping from the vehicle or utility trailer.
(Ord. 2026-009, passed 6-16-2026) Penalty, see §
50.99
§ 50.12 RECYCLING.
   Offering recycling services for items such as glass, plastics, cans, paper
and cardboard is required for any contractor. Non-recyclables shall not be
placed in the approved container for recycling. The contractor shall publish
and provide a list of all approved recycling items that are acceptable for
collection.
(Ord. 2026-009, passed 6-16-2026)
§ 50.13 INSPECTORS.
   The City Council shall have power to employ one or more Code Enforcement
Officers to ensure proper enforcement of the provisions of this chapter.
(Ord. 2026-009, passed 6-16-2026)
§ 50.14 ENFORCEMENT.
   For the purpose of enforcing the provisions of this ordinance and other
ordinances pertaining to the health and sanitation of the inhabitants of the
city, the health officers and employees of the county are hereby granted full
power and authority to act within said city and to perform all and singular the
duties of such health units on behalf of the city which they have under the
laws of the state and the rules and regulations of the State Board of Health.
(Ord. 2026-009, passed 6-16-2026)
§ 50.99 PENALTY.
   Any individual found to be in violation of this chapter or disposing of
solid waste in a manner not permitted by this code or specifically authorized
by state law shall be guilty of a misdemeanor violation and subject to a fine
of no greater than the sum of $500 for any one specified offense or violation,
double that sum for each repetition of the offense or violation. If the
prohibited offense is, by its nature, continuous or ongoing in respect to time,
the fine or penalty for allowing the continuance thereof, in violation of these
regulations or ordinance, shall not exceed $250 for each day that it may be
unlawfully continued.
(Ord. 2026-009, passed 6-16-2026)
