§ 112.01 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly requires a different meaning.
   OWNER-OCCUPIED, SHORT-TERM RENTAL. The owner of the residential dwelling
unit occupies a portion of the structure containing the short-term rental at
the same time as the short-term rental guests.
   SHORT-TERM RENTAL. A residential dwelling unit, portion of a dwelling unit,
or bedroom(s) within a residential dwelling unit, leased or rented to guests
for a period of less than 30 consecutive days. For purposes of this regulation,
the term SHORT-TERM RENTAL shall not include properties or units subject to
timeshare arrangements, unless those timeshare units are being rented on a
short-term basis to non-timeshare property owners and comply with §
112.08 of this chapter.
   TIMESHARE. Estate plans where shared ownership of dwelling units, as
controlled by A.C.A. §§ 18-14-301 et seq., are managed by a supervisory
authority to control the aspects of the timeshare plan, including the
promotion, advertising and contracting.
(Ord. 2025-004, passed 10-16-2025)
§ 112.02 PERMIT REQUIRED.
   (A)   A short-t erm rental shall not be operated, except upon issuance of a
conditional use permit for short-term rental by the city’s Planning Commission,
in accordance with the requirements of §§
152.125 through
152.128 of this code of ordinances. Said permit is non-transferable. Said
permit shall be subject to review along with a business license renewal for any
violations to these conditions, planning and zoning regulations and all other
ordinances, codes and regulations of the city.
   (B)   Said permit shall be effective so long as compliance has been
satisfactory and the business or individual to which the conditional use permit
was issued maintains a city business license and a City Advertising and
Promotion Commission tax number. The conditional use permit for short-term
rental may be revoked at any time by city officials or any court of competent
jurisdiction, for violations.
   (C)   Notwithstanding any requirement of these conditions to the contrary,
properties within the city for which a building permit has been issued may,
prior to the issuance of a certificate of occupancy, be granted an initial
conditional use permit for short-term rental as provided herein upon
application. Said permit shall be counted toward the maximum permits to be
issued as provided in §
112.07 of this chapter. Any such property shall comply with all other
applicable requirements, including the payment of an application fee. The
granting of a conditional use permit for short-term rental pursuant to this
division (C) shall not be deemed to permit occupancy of a structure prior to
the issuance of a certificate of occupancy, nor shall it be deemed to supersede
or abrogate any requirements of the state’s Fire Prevention Code with regard to
construction.
(Ord. 2025-004, passed 10-16-2025) Penalty, see §
112.99
§ 112.03 PERMIT APPLICATION AND RENEWAL.
   (A)   An application for the issuance of a conditional use permit for short-
term rental shall be on a form approved by the Mayor or the Mayor’s designee.
   (B)   The application shall require the applicant to provide the following:
      (1)   The address of the short-term rental property; the name, address,
telephone number and email address of the property owner and, if different from
the property owner, the name, address, telephone number and email address of a
person who shall serve as a local point of contact who shall reside within 50
miles of the short-term rental property;
      (2)   Proof that the applicant is in compliance with the state, county
and city for collection of state, county and local sales taxes;
      (3)   Proof that an A&P tax permit application has been submitted with
the initial conditional use permit for short-term rental application;
      (4)   Proof that the applicant is in compliance with Chapter 50 of this
code of ordinances for trash collection at the short-term rental address;
      (5)   Proof that a business license application has been submitted with
the initial conditional use permit for short-term rental application;
      (6)   A renewal application for conditional use permit for short-term
rental shall be submitted with the business license renewal application; and
      (7)   (a)   Short-term rental not owner-occupied: payment of a one-time
conditional use application fee of $150; and
         (b)   Short-term rental owner-occupied: payment of a one-time
conditional use application fee of $50.
(Ord. 2025-004, passed 10-16-2025)
§ 112.04 CONDITIONS PRECEDENT TO PERMIT ISSUANCE.
   Upon receipt of an application fulfilling the requirements of §
112.03 of this chapter, and prior to conditional use permit for short-term
rental issuance, the applicant shall agree that:
   (A)   In the event that a safety complaint is made against the property, an
inspection performed by the City Code Enforcement Officer, City Building
Inspector, Fire Chief and/or law enforcement will be required;
   (B)   The short-term rental shall have posted within the structure, in a
clearly visible location, a copy of the conditional use permit for short-term
rental, the contact information, to include a telephone number and address for
the local point of contact for the short-term rental. Additionally, recommended
placed in an appropriately labeled binder, a copy of
Chapter 91 of this code of ordinances, the requirements of §§
91.09(G) and
152.195(B)(2)(b) and a notice to all persons that guests, local points of
contact or property owners may be cited and fined for violations of applicable
city ordinances; and
   (C)   In addition to the restrictions defined in §
152.195 of this code of ordinances, due to the narrow width of the streets/
roads, on-street parking is strictly prohibited. The entirety of the paved
surface of a street is considered traffic lanes. Parking in the street right-
of-way is limited to the rental property owner’s lot. Parking on adjacent
vacant lots, whether owned by the rental property owner or not, is prohibited.
(Ord. 2025-004, passed 10-16-2025) Penalty, see §
112.99
§ 112.05 OCCUPANCY LIMITS AND RESTRICTIONS.
   At no time shall short-term rentals have, or be offered for rent as having,
an occupancy greater than three guests per bedroom, plus an additional two
guests. The number of bedrooms shall be determined in the same manner as
provided in the state’s Fire Prevention Code.
(Ord. 2025-004, passed 10-16-2025) Penalty, see §
112.99
§ 112.06 VIOLATIONS.
   (A)   The city shall have authority to seek legal and equitable relief from
any court of competent jurisdiction for violations of the provisions of this
chapter.
   (B)   No applicant for property subject to a conditional use permit for
short-term rental revocation shall be granted another conditional use permit
for short-term rental for a period of 12 months from the date of any
conditional use permit for short-term rental revocation.
(Ord. 2025-004, passed 10-16-2025) Penalty, see §
112.99
§ 112.07 MAXIMUM NUMBER OF ACTIVE PERMITS ISSUED.
   The city has not established a limit on the total number of conditional use
permits for short-term rental.
(Ord. 2025-004, passed 10-16-2025)
§ 112.08 TIMESHARES.
   Timeshare units that are being rented on a short-term basis to non-timeshare
property owners through the timeshare supervisory authority are covered by this
chapter, with the following provisions.
   (A)   One conditional use permit for short-term rental for timeshared
properties may be issued by the city’s Planning Commission to the timeshare
supervisory authority, covering each and every unit within the timeshared
property.
   (B)   Section
112.03 of this chapter applies to the timeshare supervisory authority and §
112.03(B)(7) shall be replaced with the following: payment of a one-time
conditional use permit for short-term rental application fee of $500 and does
not expire, subject to the conditions in §
112.02(A) and (B) of this chapter.
   (C)   Section
112.04 of this chapter shall be replaced for short-term rental of timeshare
units with the following: (1) A condition of granting the conditional use
permit for short-term rental requires that the timeshare supervisor authority
shall provide proof that the applicant has met the requirements of the state,
county and city for collection of state, county and local advertising and
promotion taxes; and (2) a condition of granting the conditional use permit for
short-term rental requires that the timeshare supervisory authority provide the
city’s Planning Commission with a copy of its “Rules of Conduct” for short-term
rentals. The “Rules of Conduct” must be satisfactory to the city’s Planning
Commission, in its sole discretion, as a condition of approval of granting the
permit.
   (D)   Section
112.05 of this chapter applies to short-term rental of timeshare units.
   (E)   Section
112.07 of this chapter shall not count short-term rental permits for timeshare
short-term rentals toward the maximum number allowed in the city.
(Ord. 2025-004, passed 10-16-2025)
§ 112.09 FUTURE MODIFICATIONS.
   (A)   These conditions may be modified from time to time by the City Council
and shall be distributed, by first class mail when modified, to each current
conditional use permit for short-term rental grantee.
   (B)   These conditions are the minimum requirements for the city’s Planning
Commission (HICPC) to be used for considering a conditional use application for
short-term rental. The HICPC may impose additional application specific
conditions, in its sole discretion, to apply when granting a conditional use
permit for short-term rental.
(Ord. 2025-004, passed 10-16-2025)
§ 112.10 CONDITIONAL USE FOR SHORT-TERM RENTAL.
   Conditional use for short-term rental permits that have been issued/renewed
prior to the effective date of this chapter shall remain effective until
renewed, subject to the conditions in §
112.02 of this chapter.
(Ord. 2025-004, passed 10-16-2025)
§ 112.99 PENALTY.
   Any person operating a short-term rental in violation of this chapter shall,
for a first offense, be fined not less than $250, nor more than $500. For a
second offense, a violator shall be fined an amount not less than $500, nor
more than $1,000. For a third or subsequent offense, in addition to a fine of
not less than $1,000, nor more than the maximum fine allowed by state law, any
conditional use permit for short-term rental issued pursuant to the ordinance
from which this chapter is derived shall be revoked by the court. For purposes
of this chapter, a PERSON OPERATING A SHORT-TERM RENTAL shall be the owner, the
conditional use permit for short-term rental applicant, or the local point of
contact required by §
112.03 of this chapter.
(Ord. 2025-004, passed 10-16-2025)

CHAPTER 150: PLANS ADOPTED
Section
   
150.01   Master Street Plan adopted
   
150.02   Land Use Plan adopted
§ 150.01 MASTER STREET PLAN ADOPTED.
   (A)   The map entitled “Holiday Island Road and Street Map”, which is
attached to the ordinance codified herein and made a part hereof, and the
description thereof, which follows, be and are hereby approved and adopted. The
City Recorder-Treasurer shall maintain two signed copies and a digital image
for public inspection.
   (B)   The Master Street Plan designates the following: located all within
the municipal limits of the city and more particularly described as follows.
      (1)   Definition of “road” or “street”. For the purpose of this section
and the map attached to the ordinance codified herein, the words STREET or ROAD
shall mean all roadways, public and private, open for general public travel.
Access drives to apartment and commercial complexes shall not be considered as
PRIVATE STREETS.
      (2)   Official map. The city’s Road and Street Map shall identify all
named road and street names and their relative locations. Designations of main/
primary routes shall be identified.
      (3)   Street or road names on preliminary plat. Street or road names
shall be shown on the preliminary plat of any large scale development or
planned unit development. The street names shown on the final plat shall be
consistent with those shown on the preliminary plat.
      (4)   Updating. The official Road and Street Map shall be updated by the
HICPC and recommended for City Council adoption upon final plat approval of any
plat, large scale development or planned unit development.
      (5)   Characteristics. Roads hard surfaced with chip-and-seal or asphalt
shall be of a thickness deemed necessary for the characteristics of the
substrate and of width suited to the road base width as originally constructed.
Concrete paving of streets shall include curbs. New roads or streets shall have
a driving surface of no less than 18 feet wide.
      (6)   Function/use. Roads and streets shall be open for general public
travel. Long-term or regularly-frequent parking of vehicles, trailers,
equipment or materials on the roads and streets is prohibited.
(Ord. 2021-011, passed 10-19-2021)
§ 150.02 LAND USE PLAN ADOPTED.
   The plan entitled “City of Holiday Island Land Use Plan” is hereby approved
and adopted. The City Recorder-Treasurer shall maintain two signed copies and a
digital image for public inspection.
(Ord. 2021-012, passed 10-19-2021)

GENERAL PROVISIONS
§ 151.01 BUILDING INSPECTOR.
   (A)   The provisions of this code shall be administered by the Building
Inspector, which shall be a person or persons designated by the Mayor and
approved by the City Council. The Building Inspector may be provided with the
assistance of such other people as directed by the Mayor. Decisions by the
Building Inspector may be appealed to the City Council. The Building Inspector
shall keep records of all permits issued or denied, all certificates of
occupancy issued or denied, records of all inspections and all violation
complaints received along with action taken on violation complaints.
   (B)   The Building Inspector shall enforce the provisions of this code, and
in addition shall have authority to:
      (1)   Examine and approve any application pertaining to the use of
buildings, signs or structures to determine if the application conforms to the
provisions of this code;
      (2)   Issue or deny building permits and certificates of occupancy based
upon compliance with this code and other applicable codes. Written notice
stating the reasons for denial will be provided with any building permit or
certificate of occupancy that is denied;
      (3)   Conduct inspections of buildings, structures and use of land as is
necessary to determine compliance with the regulations of this code; and
      (4)   Revoke approvals where provisions of this code are being violated.
   (C)   It shall be unlawful for the Building Inspector to engage in the
business of the sale, installation or maintenance of construction materials or
equipment, either directly or indirectly, and he or she shall have no financial
interest in any concern engaged in such business in the city, at any time while
holding such office.
(Ord. 2026-008, passed 5-26-2026)
§ 151.02 BUILDING CODE.
   (A)   All fabrication, erection, construction, enlargement, alteration or
repairs of buildings or structures requires a building permit and shall meet
the state’s Fire Prevention Code, or other applicable state and city building
codes, current editions, including the State Gas Code, State Mechanical Code,
State Plumbing Code, Arkansas (National) Electrical Code, State Energy Code,
International Residential Code (IRC) 2021 edition and the county.
   (B)   In the event of any conflict between the standards set forth in any of
the publications, the more restrictive shall control.
(Ord. 2026-008, passed 5-26-2026)
§ 151.03 EXTENSIONS, INTERIOR REMODEL; OTHER.
   The following conditions require a building permit:
   (A)   Any addition including decks and porches that changes the footprint of
the structure;
   (B)   Repair or replacement of any deck more than 32 inches above the
ground;
   (C)   Any interior remodeling that involves adding new plumbing, new
electrical circuits or adding load to an existing electrical circuit or moving
or removing a load bearing wall;
   (D)   Any structural changes to an exterior wall or roof (examples: adding a
dormer in the roof, changing the pitch of the roof or widening a doorway or
window);
   (E)   Detached accessory buildings exceeding 100 square feet in area; and
   (F)   Adding solar panels, fireplaces, swimming pools or a backup generator.
(Ord. 2026-008, passed 5-26-2026)
§ 151.04 INSPECTIONS.
   (A)   Access for inspection. The Building Inspector, or his or her assistant
and other persons authorized by the Building Inspector, is hereby granted the
authority to enter upon all property and all buildings in the city in the
performance of his or her duties between the hours of 8:00 a.m. and 5:00 p.m.
daily, except that in an emergency, the Building Inspector may enter buildings
for such purposes at other than the above designated hours, and may disseminate
information relative to the provisions of this chapter.
   (B)   Inspection; plumbing.
      (1)   There shall be a minimum of three inspections by the Building
Plumbing Inspector when plumbing is installed in any building. The first such
inspection to be known as the rough-in inspection, the second to be known as
the top-out inspection and the third as the final inspection.
         (a)   The “rough-in” inspection is to be made when the sewer drainage
or water piping is installed, but before concealing the plumbing work. This
includes a shower pan leak test.
         (b)   Top out inspection consists of the venting of the sewer drains.
         (c)   The “final” plumbing inspection is to be made when all plumbing
fixtures are connected and the plumbing job is completed.
      (2)   The Building Inspector shall have the legal right to inspect and
test an existing plumbing or drainage installation when he or she has reason to
believe that such system is not safe for the use it is intended. When such
inspections or tests indicate a faulty or unsafe system, the owner shall
recondition or replace such system in order to render it safe.
      (3)   If any plumbing work or part thereof which is installed, or altered
or repaired, is covered before being inspected by the Building Inspector, it
shall be uncovered for inspection after notice to uncover the work has been
issued to the responsible person by the Building Inspector.
   (C)   Inspection – electrical.
      (1)   When the electrical equipment is found by the Building Electrical
Inspector to be defective or defectively installed, the person, firm or
corporation responsible for the electrical equipment shall be notified in
writing and shall make any change or repairs required in the judgment of the
Building Electrical Inspector to place such equipment in safe and proper
condition and if such work is not completed in five days or such longer period
as may be specified by the Building Electrical Inspector in said notice, the
Building Electrical Inspector shall have the authority to disconnect or order
discontinuance of electrical service to said electrical equipment.
      (2)   In cases of emergency where electrical equipment may interfere with
the work of the Fire Department, the Building Electrical Inspector shall have
the authority to immediately disconnect or cause the disconnection of any
electrical equipment or electrical service thereto.
      (3)   The Building Electrical Inspector is authorized to inspect and re-
inspect all wires and apparatus within the city, previous to, during and after
its installation, to ascertain and determine whether the same is properly done
and free from danger and defects, and is hereby granted the power and authority
to order removed any obstructions which may prevent a perfect, complete and
thorough inspection of the current carrying conductors, and no electrical
wiring in new construction shall be covered or concealed until inspection has
been made.
      (4)   There shall be a minimum of two inspections by the Building
Electrical Inspector when electrical is installed in any building. The first
such inspection to be known as the rough in inspection, the second to be known
as the final inspection.
         (a)   The “rough in” inspection is to be made when all of the wiring
including sources, outlets and fixtures are installed, but before concealing
the wiring.
         (b)   The final inspection will be done upon completion of work.
      (5)   Since the city uses either a State Electrical Inspector or a
licensed electrical inspector from a neighboring community to complete the
inspections, a minimum of 48 hours’ notice to the city’s Building Inspector is
required for any electrical inspection.
   (D)   Footings, foundations and retaining walls.
      (1)   The Building Inspector is authorized to inspect all footings,
foundation walls and retaining walls. The inspection shall include, but is not
limited to, footing bedding material, capability of footings to carry the
intended load and proper reinforcing. There shall be a minimum of two
inspections:
         (a)   When footings are formed but prior to pouring concrete; and
         (b)   When walls are formed but prior to pouring concrete.
      (2)   Prior to pouring concrete that by this code requires steel
reinforcement, pictures must be taken of the steel on chairs for the Building
Inspector.
(Ord. 2026-008, passed 5-26-2026)
§ 151.05 WATER SYSTEM REQUIREMENTS.
   (A)   No water meter, other than a construction meter, may be installed or
connected to the the city’s water system without an approved building permit,
issued by the city’s Building Inspector and, until the plumbing has been
certified by the appropriate authority having jurisdiction over the project and
the Water Department.
   (B)   (1)   The city’s water and sewer systems are approved by the state’s
Department of Health. No system may be connected to the water and sewer system
unless it meets the applicable codes and regulations of the state’s Department
of Health and the requirements of the city.
      (2)   The applicant is responsible for meeting these requirements and
providing evidence of approval to the city’s Building Inspector.
   (C)   It shall be unlawful for any property owner to construct or maintain
any privy, privy vault, septic tank, cesspool or other facility intended or
used for the disposal of sewage without authorization. No private wells are
allowed within the corporate limits of the city except as allowed in the
Chapter 152.
(Ord. 2026-008, passed 5-26-2026)
§ 151.06 CONSTRUCTION GENERALLY.
   In addition to the standards set forth in the publications set forth in §
151.02:
   (A)   All water lines from the meter to the structure must be placed in
protective sleeves. A tracer wire in a blue jacket must be placed in the trench
along side the water line;
   (B)   Footings, stem walls and concrete flatwork:
      (1)   Due to the potential for subgrade material in the city to move and
settle, footing and stem wall reinforcing must be per I.R.C. § R403.1.3
regardless of seismic zone; or
      (2)   Due to the potential for subgrade material in Holiday Island to
move and settle, all concrete flat work including drives, porches and patios,
shall have rebar or wire mesh reinforcement placed in the approximate middle
third of the slab once poured. To ensure proper location, the reinforcement
material must be placed on bolsters or chairs. The type of reinforcement and
bolsters/chairs to be used must be specified on the architectural drawings.
Expansion joints must be per code.
   (C)   Service entrance cable shall not be used from the meter to the
electric panel;
   (D)   (1)   All electrical from service meter to electrical panel shall be
in electrical PVC conduits and wire size shall be in accordance with NEC
ratings of amperage. Planking/caution tape required six inches above PVC.
      (2)   Due to the rocky terrain in the city, UFER grounding required.
   (E)   Note that, Arkansas Mechanical Code § 601.3.1 requires that heating
and air conditioning ducts be sealed as soon as installed. The use of the
heating and air conditioning systems during construction is not allowed;
   (F)   When constructing a concrete block foundation or wall, vibration or
some other means is required to assure flow of concrete fill to every course of
concrete block;
   (G)   All multi-family structures, residential or commercial, shall have a
two-hour firewall between units per applicable codes;
   (H)   Driveways, curb, gutter, ditches and runoff.
      (1)   (a)   All construction in the city shall be designed in a manner
that assures that storm water will be collected and discharged without damage
to property or peril to human safety. Storm water shall be handled by curb and
gutter with storm drainage, roadside ditches, natural or improved open
waterways or underground conduits. Detention basins may be used to retard
waterflows and protect downstream property.
         (b)   Easements for installation and maintenance of utilities and
drainage facilities are reserved as shown on the recorded plot. Within these
easements, no structure, including driveways, plantings or other material,
shall be placed or permitted to remain which may damage or interfere with the
installation and maintenance of utilities, or which may:
            1.   Change the direction of flow of drainage channels in the
easements;
            2.   Obstruct or retard the flow of water through drainage channels
within the easements;
            3.   Dam up water or redirect the flow of water unto another
property; or
            4.   Direct water to flow across the roadway.
         (c)   If it is necessary to remove plantings or materials in order to
maintain said utilities or drainage, there is no obligation by any agency to
either replace the same or to pay for the same.
      (2)   Driveway culverts and paving beyond property line to the roadway
surface must be coordinated through and approved by the Road Department.
Culverts are to be installed by the Road Department and billed to the property
owner. It is required that concrete paving includes an expansion joint or
sawcut at the property line. Sloped driveways connecting to a paved street must
be hard surfaced or have an approved alternative mediation for potential gravel
washing.
      (3)   Concrete and backfill must be sloped away from the structure.
   (I)   During construction, if a builder, contractor or property owner
develops variations or deviations from the approved plan that are considered to
be major, prior to proceeding with construction, the builder/contractor shall
be required to submit such variations or deviations to the Building Inspector
for approval. Absence of prior approval to any deviation, correction shall be
at the builder’s expense.
   (J)   Exact location of the water and sewer hookups for each lot must be
determined at the job site by the Public Works Department;
   (K)   Cross-connections: Holiday Island adheres to the Safe Drinking Water
Act of 1974 (Pub. Law No. 93-523). Toward that end, it is necessary for
backflow prevention devices to be installed to protect the public water supply
from potential contamination due to cross connection. Backflow prevention
devices shall be installed in accordance with all applicable local and state
regulations effective August 1, 2020, and federal laws;
   (L)   All lots serviced by the city’s water system are required to install
individual pressure reducers on their service lines to protect the household
plumbing from excessive pressures;
   (M)   All new construction shall be required to include an electric service
disconnect switch which shall be located immediately below the electric meter,
on the exterior portion of said construction. In multiple unit construction or
any situation using multiple meters, each meter and disconnect switch, if
installed, shall be plainly marked to indicate the unit number or load
designation;
   (N)   All structures shall have smoke detectors installed on each level as
required by code. The decision as to the location and number of detectors will
be determined by the builder/contractor and in accordance with all applicable
local, state and federal rules, laws, regulations and codes;
   (O)   All handrails on exterior decks, patios or stairs more than 30 inches
above grade shall be a minimum 36 inches high for residential construction and
a minimum of 41 inches high for commercial construction and shall be
constructed in accordance with all applicable codes and ordinances;
   (P)   The access hole to the attic crawl space must be a minimum size of 20
inches by 36 inches to allow for proper entrance for firefighting purposes;
   (Q)   No blasting is allowed within the municipal limits;
   (R)   Prior to lighting any fire, it shall be the responsibility of the
owner and/or builder to notify the office of the city’s Fire Department. No
fire, once lit, shall be left unattended. No fire, once construction begins,
shall be permitted which proposes to burn construction related items except
such items and materials as may be approved from time to time by the city’s
Fire Department. No fire shall be started during any period in which a local,
county or state burn ban has been activated. No construction materials shall be
burned unless water is available at the job site. The property owner and/or the
contractor shall be responsible for any and all damage caused by any such fire;
   (S)   Unless other arrangements have been made at or prior to the
application for a building permit, and such arrangements shall have been
approved, in writing, by the Building Inspector, all construction sites shall
have portable restroom facilities available on site. Said facilities shall be
in place within seven calendar days of the activation of a permit. Each
construction site shall have on site at all times a trash container sufficient
in size to contain all consumer waste brought to or created on the site. The
container shall be emptied at sufficient intervals as needed;
   (T)   Because of the wooded nature of the city, it is recommended that
contractors and property owners consider fireproof or fire-retardant roofing
materials. Wood shakes and wood shingles are prohibited;
   (U)   Plans for constructing retaining walls between the property line and
the structure must be approved by the Building Inspector prior to the start of
construction;
   (V)   At the discretion of the Building Inspector, engineered soil
compaction specifications and measurements may be required where significant
fill has been added to the construction area;
   (W)   Brush piles left from clearing the lot must be removed before footings
are poured. When construction is complete, the construction site and lot shall
be cleaned. The area cleaned shall include all ditches and rights-of-ways.
Cleaning shall include removal of all trash, rubbish, building materials,
construction equipment and waste, including concrete.
(Ord. 2026-008, passed 5-26-2026) Penalty, see §
151.99
§ 151.07 MANUFACTURED HOMES.
   (A)   Installation of a mobile home or replacement of a mobile home with
another mobile home is prohibited. (See definition of “mobile home”.)
   (B)   Installation of a manufactured home with a manufacturing model year in
excess of ten years old is prohibited without review by the Building Inspector
and the City Council.
   (C)   Installation of a new or used manufactured (see definition of
“manufactured home”) home must be done in compliance with A.C.A. §§ 20-25-101
et seq. and “Rules Including General Installation and Anchoring
Specifications”, June 14, 2024, or most recent edition. Installation must be
done by an authorized installer. AUTHORIZED INSTALLER means any person or
employee approved, certified, licensed by or hired by the Director of the
state’s Manufactured Home Commission to perform inspection services.
   (D)   No person shall alter or cause to be altered any manufactured home to
which a label has been affixed if the alteration or conversion causes the
manufactured home to be in violation of the Federal Manufactured Home
Construction and Safety Standards (A.C.A. § 14-56-204).
   (E)   For the purpose of this section, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly requires a different meaning.
      FEDERAL STANDARDS. The federal Manufactured Home Construction and Safety
Standards, 24 C.F.R. pt. 3280, promulgated by the United States Department of
Housing and Urban Development under the authority of 42 U.S.C. §§ 5401 et seq.,
as it existed on January 1, 1976.
      MANUFACTURED HOME. A dwelling unit constructed in a factory in accordance
with the federal standards and meeting the definitions set forth in the federal
standards and under A.C.A. § 20-25-102.
      MOBILE HOME. A dwelling unit constructed in a factory before the
enactment of the federal standards (A.C.A. § 14-54-1602).
   (F)   An application for installing a used manufactured home in the city, in
addition to the plat showing the proposed location and proof of ownership of
the lot, requires:
      (1)   A certificate from the County Treasurer from where the manufactured
home is being relocated stating that there are no unpaid taxes on the property;
      (2)   A certificate of title, a copy of a certificate of title or a copy
of an application for a certificate of title submitted to the state’s
Department of Motor Vehicles; and
      (3)   A copy of the license of the mover/installer of the manufactured
home.
(Ord. 2026-008, passed 5-26-2026) Penalty, see §
151.99
§ 151.08 CONTAINER HOMES.
   Use of intermodal shipping containers for residential construction must
conform to the Internal Revenue Code and International Building Code
requirements as well as other state and local building codes. These building
codes will be strictly enforced and require extensive documentation that must
be provided by a licensed engineer. Refer to IBC 3115.
(Ord. 2026-008, passed 5-26-2026)
§ 151.09 RESIDENTIAL CONSTRUCTION GENERALLY.
   (A)   General.
      (1)   Miscellaneous types of construction and occupancy such as Quonset
hut, yurt, tent, camper, RV or tree houses are strictly prohibited as they do
not comply with the International Building or Residential Codes.
      (2)   A non-residential structure (garage, carport or accessory building
or structure) is not allowed on an R1 residential lot unless there is a
residential structure there first or there is a common wall, foundation, roof
or other inseparable feature with a residential structure on an adjacent lot
with the same owner or if a Conditional Use Permit has been issued by the
city’s Planning Commission.
      (3)   No outside lights shall be installed on public property or right-
of-way in residential units without a permit. The minimum distance between such
lights shall be 300 feet.
      (4)   Street address numbers shall be installed on the property where
they are visible from the street and in accordance with United States Post
Office requirements.
      (5)   All fences shall be constructed of brick, stone, decorative wood,
decorative metal or decorative synthetic materials. No wire fences, except
chain link, shall be permitted. No fence shall be erected upon the front yard
portion of any lot such that it extends beyond the front of the residential
structure. No fence shall be placed upon any lot, or any portion thereof, which
will interfere with the line of sight of vehicular traffic at any intersection.
      (6)   Orientation of R1 residences must be such that the front entrance
generally faces the street frontage. Front entrance(s) must be directly
accessible from the street frontage. The platting of R2 and R3 residences must
be approved by the City Planning Commission if not orientated as required for
R1 residences. R2 and R3 front entrances must be accessible from the street
frontage.
   (B)   Certificate of occupancy.
      (1)   In accordance with IBC §
110.01, no building that will be occupied for residential or commercial
purposes that has been erected or structurally altered shall be used, occupied
or changed in use until a certificate of occupancy shall have been issued by
the Building Inspector. The Building Inspector shall maintain a record of all
certificates of occupancy. Building permits issued for electrical, plumbing,
decks, porches, detached garages, accessory buildings and solar panels and the
like only require passing inspection to be closed out.
      (2)   A certificate of occupancy may be revoked by the Building Inspector
when it is found that the building or land does not conform to the use or
condition, if any, in the certificate.
      (3)   Contractors or owners who complete jobs without requesting a final
inspection and certificate of occupancy shall be subject to the penalties per §
151.99.
(Ord. 2026-008, passed 5-26-2026) Penalty, see §
151.99
§ 151.10 COMPLIANCE; ENFORCEMENT RESPONSIBILITIES.
   (A)   Acceptance. Each property owner and builder contractor, by virtue of
acceptance and signing of the building permit, does agree to abide by the rules
and regulations set forth in this document, and, in the event of a violation
thereof, do agree to submit themselves to the jurisdiction of the courts of the
county.
   (B)   Enforcement responsibilities.
      (1)   If the Building Inspector or Code Enforcement Officer shall find
that the provisions of these regulations are being violated, he or she shall
notify the person responsible for such violations in writing, indicating the
nature of the violation and ordering the action necessary to correct it. A
“stop construction” notice shall be posted at the building site if violations
are not remedied before any additional work is performed on the site.
      (2)   Should the person responsible for such violations fail to take the
necessary action to correct it, the Building Inspector shall notify the City
Attorney, and the City Attorney shall within ten days apply for an injunction,
mandamus or other process to prevent, enjoin, abate or remove said violation to
these regulations.
   (C)   Appeal. Should the contractor and Building Inspector fail to agree on
the validity of a violation, the contractor or property owner can appeal the
decision to the City Council.
(Ord. 2026-008, passed 5-26-2026)
§ 151.11 STREET OPENINGS.
   All openings made in the public streets or alleys to install plumbing must
be made by the responsible utility and shall comply to any street cut ordinance
in effect.
(Ord. 2026-008, passed 5-26-2026)
§ 151.12 LIABILITY.
   Any officer, employee or person, charged with the enforcement of this code,
acting for the city in the discharge of his or her duties, shall not thereby
render himself or herself liable personally, and he or she is hereby relieved
from all personal liability for any damage that may accrue to persons or
property as a result of any act required or permitted in the discharge of his
or her duties. Any suit brought against any officer or employee because of such
act performed by him or her in the enforcement of any provision of this code
shall be defended by the City Attorney or legal representative of the city.
(Ord. 2026-008, passed 5-26-2026)
BUILDING PERMITS
§ 151.25 PERMIT ISSUANCE.
   (A)   Building permits will be issued in accordance with A.C.A. §§ 14-1-501
et seq. relative to timely response to building permit applications, issuance
of building permits and the rights of owners or contractors to enlist at their
expense the services of a qualified private professional to review the building
permit documents and conduct on-site inspections. PRIVATE PROFESSIONAL PROVIDER
means one of the following who is not an employee of or otherwise affiliated
with the person engaged in the construction project to be reviewed or
inspected:
      (1)   A professional engineer as defined in A.C.A. § 17-30-101;
      (2)   A registered architect as defined in A.C.A. § 17-15-102; or
      (3)   A person licensed, certified or registered by the applicable agency
or body to conduct the type of plan review or inspection covered under this
section.
   (B)   The city will issue a building permit for either the new construction,
additions or renovation of a building or structure within the corporate limits
of the city only when the application has been submitted and approved by the
Building Inspector as meeting the requirements of these regulations. No
building permit shall be issued for the construction of any building or
structure located on a lot or parcel subdivided or sold in violation of the
provisions of the Planning and Zoning regulations, nor shall the municipality
have any obligation to issue certificates of occupancy or to extend utility
services to any parcel created in violation of those regulations.
   (C)   Existing parcels and lots cannot be subdivided or zoned without being
submitted and approved by the City Planning Commission in accordance with the
planning and zoning regulations in Chapter 152.
(Ord. 2026-008, passed 5-26-2026)
§ 151.26 PERMIT APPLICATION.
   (A)   All applications for building permits shall be provided to the
Building Inspector and be accompanied by two sets of the following:
      (1)   A plot plan, drawn to scale on a copy of an original survey and
stamped by a licensed surveyor, showing the legal description of the property
and the location of the structure to be erected, and indicating the setback
from the property lines. The location and type of the planned driveway shall be
shown on the plot plan. A plot plan is only required for new buildings or
additions to existing buildings including garages, decks, porches, pergolas or
anything that changes the footprint of an existing structure;
      (2)   Per I.R.C. § 106, drawings and plans must be prepared by a
registered design professional (architect or engineer) as required by this
statute. Exceptions can be made by the Building Inspector if it is found that
the nature of the work and drawings and specifications submitted are sufficient
to determine compliance with this code. The minimum requirements are a
foundation plan, a drawn to scale floor plan enclosed area and power and
lighting, and all elevations showing openings; and
      (3)   License numbers for all state-licensed building contractors, master
plumbers, master electricians and heat and air contractors supervising or
performing all plumbing, electrical and heating and air conditioning work for
said construction, shall be on file with the City Building Inspector. Proof of
qualifying business license required.
   (B)   A road damage permit is required from the Suburban Improvement
District. The permit may be obtained at the HISID office located at 110
Woodsdale Drive.
(Ord. 2026-008, passed 5-26-2026)
§ 151.27 PLAN REJECTION OR APPROVAL.
   (A)   Rejection; appeals process.
      (1)   Application review. The packages will be reviewed within 24 hours
relative to §
151.26(A)(1) through (A)(3). If incomplete or inadequate, the package will be
returned to the requester with a detailed list of missing, incomplete or
inadequate documentation.
      (2)   Drawing review. Questions about building code interpretation or
application may be appealed to the State Building Inspector. However, local
requirements found in the Building Code that are more restrictive than the
state or national codes cannot be appealed.
      (3)   Appeals. In accordance with §
152.039, decisions made by the Building Inspector related to zoning including
uses of the property or setback and easement requirements may be appealed to
the Board of Zoning Adjustment per the process delineated in the zoning
regulations. Decisions made by the Building Inspector that are not related to
the zoning regulations may be appealed to the City Council.
   (B)   Approval.
      (1)   Upon approval of the application for Building Permit, one set of
stamped construction plans and the survey showing the plot plan will be
returned to the applicant, along with two copies of the Building Permit. One
copy of the permit shall be posted at the job site and visible at all times
during construction.
      (2)   Construction must be initiated within 30 days from the date of the
permit application, or the permit will be null and void and permit fees will be
forfeited. Upon the issuance of the Building Permit, construction is to be
completed within 365 consecutive calendar days. Failure to complete
construction within the timeframe constraints will require the issuance of a
Building Permit extension in the amount of $100. This extension will extend the
original permit time by 182 consecutive calendar days. A building permit can be
extended only one time after which a new building permit must be issued per the
permit fee schedule. In the event it is determined that the construction will
not be completed in the specified time, the builder shall advise the Building
Inspector at least 90 days prior to the expiration of said permit by submitting
a request for an extension of time to the Building Inspector for consideration
by the Building Inspector. At the Building Inspector’s discretion, the permit
extension fee may be waved if needed due to such things as an act of God or
availability of materials.
      (3)   Size, for the purposes of the building permit, is based upon the
number of square feet of gross heated space plus any additional habitable
living area including decks, porches, screened porches, sunrooms, garages,
carports, breezeways or similar features when located above grade.
      (4)   The Building Inspector shall issue to the property owner or builder
representing the owner a packet containing a copy of the building permit, a job
inspection card and a copy of these regulations.
      (5)   Most units in the city have a declaration of reservations. These
are binding agreements between the property owners in the unit and will be
considered during the permitting process. However, the city is not responsible
for enforcement of the declaration of reservations, nor compelled by its text.
(Ord. 2026-008, passed 5-26-2026)
§ 151.28 SOLAR INSTALLATIONS.
   (A)   A solar installation permit is required. The solar installer must be
licensed in the state to do solar installations, and work must be completed in
accordance with all applicable building and electrical codes.
   (B)   The application for the permit must include the specifications for the
system. For rooftop installations, verification is required that the roof
structure is adequate to carry the extra load. For ground level installations,
a plot plan is required showing the location of the panels relative to
applicable zoning regulations (setbacks, easements and front yard
restrictions).
   (C)   Electrical work must be completed by an electrician licensed in the
state and inspected by an electrical inspector licensed in the state. A “final
state” inspection is required to be signed by the inspector and the electrician
before Carroll Electric will permit “going online”.
(Ord. 2026-008, passed 5-26-2026)
§ 151.29 SWIMMING POOLS.
   (A)   General.
      (1)   The installation of a swimming pool requires a permit. For above
ground pools, an electrical inspection by a licensed electrician is required.
      (2)   For in-ground pools, if a retaining wall is being incorporated, the
construction plans for the retaining wall must be approved by the Building
Inspector. All electrical must be approved by a licensed electrician before
covering any wiring. Drains for in-ground and above ground pools must be
connected to the HISID sewer system in accordance with all applicable plumbing
codes and regulations.
      (3)   The following is provided for reference and is not a substitute for
any applicable code.
   (B)   Grounding.
      (1)   All electrical equipment for swimming pools must be grounded and
connected by wiring methods in accordance with NFPA 70, National Electric Code®
(NEC®).
      (2)   The following must be grounded:
         (a)   All electrical equipment associated with the circulation system;
         (b)   All electrical equipment located within five feet of the inside
wall of the pool water;
         (c)   All through-wall lighting assemblies and underwater luminaires;
and
         (d)   Panelboards that supply electricity to equipment associated with
the pool.
      (3)   Ground-fault circuit interrupters (GFCIs) are required with
transformer and power supply enclosures, junction boxes and pool motors.
      (4)   Grounding and bonding terminals should be identified as being used
for wet and corrosive environments.
      (5)   Grounding and bonding connections should be made of copper, copper
alloy or stainless steel. They also should be listed for direct burial.
      (6)   Luminaries and related equipment should also be grounded. All
lighting assemblies and luminaires must be connected to an insulated copper
grounding conductor not smaller than 12 AWG. Where a non-metallic conduit is
installed, the installation of an eight AWG insulated copper bonding jumper may
be required in the conduit. Wet-niche luminaires supplied by a flexible cord
must have all exposed non-current-carrying metal parts grounded.
      (7)   An equipment-grounding conductor should be installed with the
feeder conductors between the grounding terminal of the pool equipment
panelboard and the grounding terminal of the applicable service equipment.
   (C)   Bonding.
      (1)   Bonding is the joining of metallic parts to form an electrically
conductive path that will result in electrical continuity between components to
ensure that the electrical potential will be the same throughout.
      (2)   The following parts of pools, spas and hot tubs must be bonded
together using conductors of at least eight AWG or using rigid metal conduit.
      (3)   All metallic parts of the pool’s structure, including reinforcing
metal, must be bonded together using solid copper conductors (insulated,
covered or bare), and at least eight AWG, or with rigid metal conduit of brass
or other corrosion-resistant metal. Connections of bonded parts must be made in
accordance with the NEC® (refer to § 250.8).
      (4)   All underwater metal-formed lighting shells must be bonded, as well
as all metal fittings within or attached to the pool structure. Metal parts of
electrical equipment related to the water circulation system, including pumps,
motors, metal parts of pool covers and associated equipment must be bonded. All
fixed metal parts must be bonded, including metal-sheathed cables and raceways,
metal piping, metal awnings, metal fences, metal doors and metal window frames.
      (5)   The following is a general list of the items that require
equipotential bonding:
         (a)   Conductive pool shells, including poured concrete, sprayed
concrete and concrete block with painted or plastered coatings;
         (b)   Structural reinforcing steel;
         (c)   Perimeter surfaces that extend three feet horizontally beyond
the inside walls of the pool, spa or hot tub. A perimeter surface that extends
less than three feet and is separated from the pool by a barrier shall require
equipotential bonding on the pool side of the barrier. Bonding to perimeter
surfaces shall be provided and be attached to the pool, spa and hot tub
reinforcing steel or copper conductor grid at a minimum of four points around
the pool, spa or hot tub;
         (d)   Metallic components;
         (e)   Underwater lighting;
         (f)   Metal fittings;
         (g)   Electrical equipment; and
         (h)   All fixed metal parts.
      (6)   The perimeter surface that is considered to be bonded is the area
that extends three feet horizontally beyond the inside walls of the pool and
includes the unpaved surfaces and other types of paving. Bonding to perimeter
surfaces can be attached to the pool reinforcing steel or copper conductor grid
at a minimum of four points spaced around the pool’s perimeter.
   (D)   Summary.
      (1)   The electrical equipment for swimming pools must be grounded and
connected by wiring methods in accordance with NFPA 70, National Electric
Code®.
      (2)   In addition to grounding, bonding is required to get all metal
parts of the electrical equipment and the non-electrical metal parts of the
pool/spa structure to attain equal electrical potential.
(Ord. 2026-008, passed 5-26-2026)

§ 151.31 VIOLATIONS.
   Fees will be doubled to the owner or the contractor for beginning
construction without a permit. Clearing and grubbing of the property is allowed
without permit. No footings or in-ground work will be allowed until the plans
have been submitted to the Building Inspector, approved by the Building
Inspector and a building permit issued for the project. No sewer and water
connections are to be made without a building permit.
(Ord. 2026-008, passed 5-26-2026)
§ 151.32 EXCEPTIONS.
   (A)   No permit is required for simple maintenance, such as replacing
faucets, sink drains, household light fixtures or replacing siding or roofing
material.
   (B)   Storage sheds and portable buildings under 100 square feet do not need
a permit.
   (C)   New fence installation requires plan approval by the Building
Inspector. A no-charge permit will be issued.
   (D)   All concrete flatwork exceeding 120 square feet and not otherwise
associated with an existing building permit requires a no-charge building
permit.
   (E)   Design and placement of hobby chicken coops must be approved by the
Building Inspector and requires a no-charge building permit.
(Ord. 2026-008, passed 5-26-2026) Penalty, see §
151.99
§ 151.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person or corporation who shall violate any of the provisions of
these regulations or fail to comply with any of the requirements, or who shall
build or alter any building in violation of the detailed statement of plans
submitted and approved hereunder shall be deemed guilty of a misdemeanor and
shall be liable to a fine of not more than $500. Each day such violation is
permitted to exist shall constitute a separate offense. The owner or owners of
any building or premises or part of a building/premises, where anything in
violation of these regulations shall be placed, or shall exist; and any
architect, builder, contractor, agent, engineer, person or other corporation
employed in connection with and who may have assisted in the commission of any
such violation, shall be deemed guilty of a separate offense and upon
conviction shall be fined.
   (B)   Repeat offences may result in a suspension of the offender’s city
business license or a permanent prohibition from obtaining future building
permits.
(Ord. 2026-008, passed 5-26-2026)

GENERAL PROVISIONS
§ 152.001 TITLE; PROVISIONS.
   This chapter shall constitute the planning and zoning regulations of the
city. It may be cited as the “zoning ordinance” or the “zoning code” or “this
chapter” and consists of the text, which follows, as well the zoning district
boundary map, entitled “City of Holiday Island, Arkansas, Zoning Map” (official
zoning map) and the “City of Holiday Island UBL (unit-block-lot) Zoning Map
Table” (official zoning map table) which are on file in the office of the
city’s Recorder-Treasurer.
(Ord. 2025-008, passed 10-16-2025; Ord. 2026-005, passed 3-17-2026; Ord. 2026-
012, passed 6-16- 2026)
§ 152.002 AUTHORITY.
   (A)   These regulations are adopted pursuant to authority granted by the
state’s General Assembly in A.C.A. Title 14, Chapter 56, Subchapter 4, as
amended.
   (B)   All membership in the various boards and commissions having authority
hereunder, acting prior to the effective date of this chapter, shall remain in
office and serve the remainder of their respective terms.
(Ord. 2025-008, passed 10-16-2025)
§ 152.003 PURPOSE.
   (A)   The planning and zoning regulations set forth herein are enacted to
aid in the implementation of the land use portion of the city’s Land Use Plan,
and to promote, in accordance with present and future needs, the safety, order,
convenience, prosperity and general welfare of the citizens. The regulations
are intended to provide for the health, safety and welfare of the public and
the orderly growth and development; for protection of the character and
stability of residential, commercial, industrial, recreational and
environmentally sensitive areas of the city; for protection of property from
blight and undue depreciation; for efficiency and economy in the process of
development for the appropriate and best use of land; for the use and occupancy
of buildings; for healthful and convenient distribution of population; for good
civic design and arrangement; and for adequate public utilities and facilities.
   (B)   The Holiday Island Suburban Improvement District is acknowledged.
      (1)   The city adopted its Planning Area Map that defined the territory
for which the city will develop and adopt land use zoning ordinances and
regulations.
      (2)   The properties within the Holiday Island Suburban Improvement
District (“HISID”) have been defined by a system of unit-block-lot or parcel
designations. It is the intent of the city that the existing lot and parcel
designations be adopted for continuity of identification.
      (3)   Most residential and commercial properties in the city are covered
by a declaration of reservations (commonly referred to as covenants) that are
unique to the unit in which the property lies. Although these covenants are
legal agreements between the unit property owners and are not binding upon the
city, it is the intent and desire of the city to preserve the available
declaration of reservations to the limits of the law placed upon municipalities
in the state and to the extent that these declarations of reservations remain
relevant.
      (4)   The city intends to develop and adopt ordinances and regulations to
work in conformity with the available declaration of reservations to the extent
possible pursuant to the state statutes.
      (5)   Lots and parcels in the various units have diverse dimensions, due
primarily to the terrain conditions upon which they are platted. There are lots
and parcels that do not fit the general description of the designated zoning
district precisely, but are included within the same zoning district to ensure
similar use as adjoining lots. The declarations of reservations include
similarly named use designations that differ from unit to unit. These
regulations will strive to align with the declaration of reservations where
applicable.
(Ord. 2025-008, passed 10-16-2025)
§ 152.004 JURISDICTION.
   (A)   The provisions of these regulations shall apply to all land, water,
buildings and structures within the corporate limits of the city as they now or
may hereafter exist.
   (B)   No action or decision by the City Council or Planning Commission
concerning any change in the zoning or use of property shall alter, limit,
impair or otherwise affect the authority, actions or responsibilities of the
Holiday Island Suburban Improvement District, or of any county or state
officials.
(Ord. 2025-008, passed 10-16-2025; Ord. 2026-010, passed 4-21-2026)
§ 152.005 NATURE AND APPLICATION.
   (A)   For the purposes stated above, the city has been divided into zoning
districts in which the regulations contained herein will govern lot coverage;
the height, area, bulk, location and size of buildings; open space; and the
uses of land, buildings and structures. In their interpretation and
application, the provisions of these regulations shall be held to be the
minimum requirements for the promotion of the public health, safety, order,
convenience, prosperity and general welfare. Whenever these requirements are at
variance with the requirements of any other lawfully adopted rules or
regulations, the most restrictive, or that imposing the higher standards, shall
govern; provided, however, that, the city shall not be responsible for
enforcing deed restrictions, restrictive covenants or declarations of
reservations.
   (B)   No land shall be used or occupied, no structure shall be erected,
moved, converted, altered, enlarged, used or occupied, and no use shall be
operated, unless it is in conformity with the regulations herein prescribed for
the district in which such structure or land is located. This provision shall
not be construed to affect any lawful uses of land or structures that exist, or
for which a lawfully issued permit has been issued, at the effective date of
these regulations.
   (C)   No proposed plat of any new subdivision of land shall hereafter be
considered for approval unless the lots within such plat equal or exceed the
minimum size and area requirements specified in the applicable zoning district
in which the land is located.
   (D)   No open space required by these regulations for a particular structure
or use shall be claimed at the same time as open space for another structure or
use.
   (E)   Dedication to public use of land shall not be a condition for any
zoning or conditional use approval.
   (F)   All structures constructed or occupied in conformance with these
regulations shall also conform to all other codes and regulations of the city.
   (G)   The provisions of these regulations are severable. If any section,
paragraph, sentence or clause shall be declared invalid, the remainder of the
regulations shall not be affected.
(Ord. 2025-008, passed 10-16-2025)
§ 152.006 RULES OF CONSTRUCTION.
   For these regulations, the following rules of construction shall apply.
   (A)   Words, phrases and terms defined herein shall be given the defined
meaning.
   (B)   Words, phrases and terms not defined herein, but in the Building Code
of the city, shall be construed as defined in such code.
   (C)   Words, phrases and terms neither defined herein, nor in the Building
Code, shall be given their usual and customary meanings, except where the
context clearly indicates a different meaning.
   (D)   In case of any difference of meaning or implication between the text
and any heading, drawing, table or figure, the text shall control.
   (E)   The particular shall control the general.
   (F)   The word “shall” is always mandatory and not discretionary. The word
“may” is permissive and not mandatory.
   (G)   Words used in the present tense include the future tense, and words
used in the future tense include the present tense.
   (H)   Words used in the singular include the plural, and words used in the
plural include the singular.
   (I)   The words “building” and “structure” are synonymous, and include any
part thereof.
   (J)   The word “person” includes individuals, firms, corporations,
associations and any other similar entities.
   (K)   The words “lot”, “parcel”, “site”, “tract” or other unit of ownership
are synonymous and may be used interchangeably.
   (L)   The word “used” shall include arranged, designed, constructed,
altered, converted, rented, leased or intended to be used.
   (M)   All public officials, bodies and agencies to which reference is made
are those of the city, unless otherwise indicated.
   (N)   Whenever reference is made to a resolution, ordinance, statute,
regulation, map or document, it shall be construed as a reference to the most
recent edition of such resolution, ordinance, statute, regulation, map or
document, unless otherwise expressly stated.
   (O)   Whenever a provision appears requiring the head of a department or
another officer or employee to perform an act or duty, that provision shall be
construed as authorizing the department head or officer or employee to delegate
the responsibility to subordinates, unless the terms of the provision specify
otherwise.
   (P)   Unless the context clearly suggests the contrary, the conjunction
“and” indicates that all connected items, conditions, provisions or events
shall apply, and the conjunction “or” indicates that one or more of the
connected items, conditions, provisions or events shall apply.
   (Q)   Every permit issued shall become invalid unless the work on the site
authorized by such permit is commenced within 180 days after its issuance, or
if the work authorized on the site by such permit is suspended or abandoned for
a period of 180 days after the time the work is commenced.
   (R)   The word CITY means the areas of jurisdiction of the CITY OF HOLIDAY
ISLAND, ARKANSAS.
   (S)   The word PERMITTED or words PERMITTED BY RIGHT mean(s) permitted
without meeting the requirements for a conditional use permit or site plan.
(Ord. 2025-008, passed 10-16-2025)
§ 152.007 DEFINITIONS.
   This section contains definitions of general terms used throughout the text
or may be added at a later date. It also contains definitions for the uses
identified in the text or intended to be used at a later date. The use
definitions are intended to be mutually exclusive, which means that uses that
are specifically defined shall not also be considered a part of a more general
definition of that use. The use “retail/service”, for example, does not include
the more specific use “convenience store”. For the purpose of this chapter, the
following definitions shall apply unless the context clearly requires a
different meaning.
   ACCESS EASEMENT. A right-of-way or parcel of land specified or set aside as
the way or means by which a piece of property is approached or entered, given
by the owner of land to another party.
   ACCESSORY BUILDINGS AND USES. A subordinate building or a portion of the
principal building, the use of which is customarily incidental to that of the
dominant use of the principal building or land. An ACCESSORY USE is one that is
customarily incidental, appropriate and subordinate to the principal use of
land and buildings. ACCESSORY BUILDINGS AND USES are located on the same lot,
or an adjoining lot, and in the same zoning district as the principal use.
ACCESSORY BUILDING may be defined as, but not limited to, fences and walls;
garages, carports and off-street parking and loading areas; gardens;
greenhouses; gates and guard houses; playhouses, patios, cabanas, porches,
gazebos and household storage buildings; radio and television receiving
antennas; recreational and play facilities for residents; storm and fallout
shelters; and other necessary and customary uses determined to be appropriate,
incidental and subordinate to the principal use on the lot.
   ACCESSORY DWELLING UNIT (ADU). A self-contained, and independently accessed,
living unit, on the same parcel as a single-family dwelling of greater square
footage, and that includes its own living, sleeping, cooking and sanitation
facilities. The ACCESSORY DWELLING UNIT may be attached, detached or internal
to the single-family dwelling. If attached or detached, the ADU shall not be
more than 75% of the square footage of the principal dwelling, or 1,000 square
feet, whichever is less.
   ADULT ENTERTAINMENT. Any adult cabaret, adult theater, adult bookstore,
adult massage establishment, model studio or sexual encounter or meditation
center which depicts or describes matters or activities relating to specified
sexual activities or specified anatomical areas.
   AGRICULTURE, ANIMAL. The use of any land for the purpose of raising
livestock.
   AGRICULTURE, CROP. The primary use of any land for the purpose of growing
plants, crops, trees and other agricultural or forestry products. This does not
include hobby gardens, as an “accessory use”, for personal consumption of the
crop.
   AGRICULTURE, FARMER’S MARKET. Local resident’s temporary produce stands
selling personally-grown fruit, vegetables, farm products or provisions
participating in outdoor markets or special events organized, administered or
approved by the city.
   AGRICULTURE, PRODUCT SALES. The retail sale of agricultural products
produced on the same site.
   AIRPORT (AIRSTRIP). An area of land or water which is used, or intended for
use, for the landing and takeoff of aircraft and any appurtenant areas which
are used, or intended for use, for airport buildings or other airport
facilities or rights-of-way, together with all airport buildings and facilities
located thereon.
   ALLEY. A narrow public way, not in excess of 20 feet, which affords a
secondary means of access to abutting properties, and not intended for general
traffic circulation.
   ANIMAL, FARM. Any animal that customarily is raised in an agricultural,
rather than urban, environment, for profit on farms and has the potential of
causing a nuisance if not properly maintained, including, but not limited to,
chickens, pigs, sheep, goats, horses, cattle, llamas, emus, ostriches, donkeys
and mules. This definition does not include hobby chicken, which is separately
defined in this section.
   ANIMAL CARE, GENERAL. A use providing animal care, veterinary services or
boarding.
   ANIMAL CARE, LIMITED. A use providing small animal (household pet) boarding
or veterinary services, with no outside runs.
   APARTMENT. A room or suite of rooms within a building with separate cooking,
bathing and sleeping facilities and intended as a single dwelling unit.
Structures containing five or more dwelling units are considered APARTMENTS.
   APPROVAL, CONDITIONAL. Approval of a plan, plat or activity by the
Commission subject to performance by the applicant of certain stipulated
conditions.
   AREA. The amount of land surface in a lot or parcel of land.
   ART DISPLAY, ORIGINAL. A hand-painted work of visual art that is either
affixed to or painted directly on the exterior wall of a structure with the
permission of the property owner. An ORIGINAL ART DISPLAY does not include:
mechanically produced or computer-generated prints or images, including, but
not limited to, digitally printed vinyl; electrical or mechanical components;
or changing image art display.
   AS-BUILT DRAWING. A document showing how a particular building and/or site
have been constructed.
   ASPHALT OR CONCRETE PLANT. An establishment engaged in the manufacture,
mixing, batching or recycling of asphalt, asphalt cement, cement or concrete
products.
   AUDITORIUM or STADIUM. An open, partially enclosed or fully enclosed
facility used or intended to be used primarily for spectator sports,
entertainment events, expositions and other public gatherings.
   AUTO WRECKING OR SALVAGE YARD. A lot, land or structure, or part thereof,
used primarily for the collecting, dismantling, storage and salvaging of
machinery or vehicles that are not in operating condition, or for the sale of
parts therefrom; or for the collecting, storage and salvage of waste paper,
scrap metal or other discard material.
   AUTOMATED TELLER MACHINE (ATM). Unmanned electronic banking facilities at
which banking transactions may be accepted and/or cash disbursements made.
   BANK or FINANCIAL INSTITUTION. Establishments engaged in deposit banking;
typically, commercial banks, savings and loans and credit unions.
   BAR (LOUNGE). An establishment that only sells/serves alcoholic drink for
consumption within the establishment. A BAR is not permitted to operate in any
district of the city.
   BASIC INDUSTRY. The first operation or operations that transform a material
from its raw state to a form suitable for fabrication.
   BED AND BREAKFAST. The use of an owner-occupied or manager-occupied
residential structure to provide temporary lodging, or lodging and meals, with
no more than 12 guest rooms.
   BEDROOM. A room for use as living quarters, within a dwelling, that includes
a closet and requisite number of exterior windows, of sufficient size and
placement, to conform to the National Fire Code.
   BILL OF ASSURANCE. A private agreement attached to a plat which establishes
property use and development rules specific to the properties in the particular
subdivision and which may be binding upon subsequent owners of the property.
   BOND. Any form of security including a cash deposit, surety bond or
instrument of credit in an amount and form satisfactory to the city.
   BUILDING. Any structure including a roof supported by walls, designed or
intended for the support, enclosure, shelter or protection of persons, animals,
chattels or property and forming a construction that is safe and stable.
   BUILDING CODE. Regulations established by local governments describing the
minimum requirements for buildings; includes foundation, roofing, plumbing,
electrical and other specifications for safety and sanitation.
   BUILDING HEIGHT. The vertical distance from highest part of the lot(s) upon
which building is constructed to the height of the highest roof surface.
   BUILDING LINES. The lines that are parallel to the front, side, or rear lot
lines of a lot at a distance equal to the minimum setback requirements, and
beyond which the vertical wall of a building or structure shall not be located
closer to said lot lines.
   BUILDING, PRINCIPAL. A building in which is conducted the principal use of
the plot on which it is situated. In any residential district, any structure
containing a dwelling unit of the largest square footage shall be deemed to be
the PRINCIPAL BUILDING on the plot on which the same is situated.
   BUILDING SETBACKS. See SETBACKS.
   CAR WASH. A facility for washing or steam cleaning passenger automobiles,
pickups, service vehicles and light trucks (including a self-service
operation), operating either as a separate facility or when installed and
operated in conjunction with another use, and which installation includes
equipment customarily associated with a CAR WASH and is installed solely for
the purpose of washing and cleaning vehicles.
   CARPORT. Space for the housing or storage of motor vehicles and enclosed on
not more than two sides by walls.
   CEMETERY. Land used, or intended to be used, for burial of the dead, whether
human or animal, including a mausoleum, columbarium or cinerarium.
   CERTIFICATE OF OCCUPANCY. Permission to occupy a building and/or property.
   CHICKEN, HOBBY. Fowl that are being raised on non-agricultural lots as a
hobby instead of as a farming operation. HOBBY CHICKENS are used for the
production of eggs, garden fertilizer and meat production or as pets for non-
commercial purposes.
   CHURCH. A building, together with its accessory buildings and uses, where
persons regularly assemble for religious worship, and which building, together
with its accessory buildings and uses, including day cares, is maintained and
controlled by a religious body organized to sustain public worship.
   CITY. Unless specified otherwise within the context of the section, the City
of Holiday Island, Arkansas.
   CLUB or LODGE. An association of persons for the promotion of some non-
profit common purpose, such as charity, literature, science, politics,
fellowship and the like, meeting periodically, and limited to members.
   CODE ENFORCEMENT OFFICER. An employee of the city designated by the Mayor to
administer and enforce this code.
   COLLEGE or UNIVERSITY. An institution of higher education offering
undergraduate or graduate degrees.
   COMMENCEMENT OF CONSTRUCTION. Any clearing of the land, excavation,
construction or other substantial action that would adversely affect the
natural environment of the site.
   COMPLETION DATE OF PROJECT. A date established by agreement between the
Planning Commission and applicant as reached at time of preliminary plat
approval.
   COMPREHENSIVE PLAN. The city’s Land Use Plan adopted 10-19-2022. May include
a composite of the mapped and written proposals, as adopted by the Planning
Commission and City Council, whether in whole or in part, recommending the
physical development of the community. Said plan may include, but not limited
to, the Planning Area Map, Land Use Plan, Master Street Plan, Master Street
Plan Map, Zoning Map and Zoning Map Table for the city.
   CONDOMINIUM. A condominium (also called a CONDO) is a large property or
complex comprised of individual units, and each unit is owned separately.
Ownership usually includes a non- exclusive interest in certain “community
property” controlled by the condominium management. A condo owner owns the air
space inside their condo, sharing an ownership interest in the community
property, such as the floor, walls, sidewalks, stairwells and exterior areas.
Condominium management is usually made up of a board of unit owners who
oversees the daily operation of the complex, such as lawn maintenance and snow
removal.
   CONSTRUCTION SALES AND SERVICE. An establishment engaged in the retail or
wholesale sale of materials used in the construction of buildings or other
structures, as well as the outdoor storage of construction equipment or
materials on lots other than construction sites. Typical uses include
lumberyards, home improvement centers, lawn and garden supply stores,
electrical, plumbing, air conditioning and heating supply stores, swimming pool
sales and construction and trade contractor storage yards.
   CONTAINER HOUSING. A six-sided steel unit, intermodal shipping container,
originally constructed as a general cargo container used for the transport of
goods and materials that have been repurposed, on or off site, in compliance
with all of the International Building Code (IBC 2021) § 3115 et seq.
requirements for habitable occupancy. CONTAINER HOUSING (“container home”) is
subject to the same setbacks, height limits, minimum square footages, building
codes and permitting requirements as a site-built residential structure.
   CONTAINER, STORAGE. A unit such as, but not limited to, intermodal shipping
container, Conex box or light freight container (pods). A reusable cargo
container of a rigid construction and rectangular configuration; fitted with
devices permitting its ready handling, particularly its transfer from one mode
of transport to another; so designed to be readily filled and emptied; intended
to contain one or more articles of cargo or bulk commodities for transportation
by water or and one or more other transport modes. The term includes completely
enclosed units, open top units, fractional height units, units incorporating
liquid or gas tanks and other variations fitting into the container system. It
does not include cylinders, drums, crates, cases, cartons, packages, sacks,
unitized loads or any other form of packaging. (29 C.F.R. § 1918.2)
   CONVENIENCE STORE. An establishment, not exceeding 3,500 square feet of
gross floor area, serving a limited market area, and engaged in the retail sale
of food, beverages, gasoline and other frequently or recurrently needed
merchandise for household or automotive use, and which may specifically include
a car wash as an accessory use.
   COUNTRY CLUB. A chartered, non-profit membership club catering primarily to
its membership, providing one or more of the following social and recreational
activities: golf, tennis, swimming, riding or outdoor recreation. Such clubs
typically include dining facilities, clubhouses, locker rooms and pro shops.
   COVENANT. A provision in a deed limiting the use of the property and
prohibiting certain uses.
   DAY CARE, GENERAL (DAY CARE CENTER). A commercial establishment where adult
day care services are provided, or where child day care services are provided
for more than eight children; with both such services to be provided pursuant
to state laws and fire codes, and in accordance with, and licensed by
appropriate state agencies.
   DAY CARE, LIMITED (DAY CARE FAMILY HOME). The operator shall reside in the
structure, and the facility residential home must conform to all codes and
regulations, both state and local, applicable thereto, with the most
restrictive regulations prevailing. Section 102.4 of the Arkansas Department of
Human Services’ Minimum Licensing Requirements for Licensed Child Care Family
